Owner scope: Employee.country means nationality; restore birth, address, picture and contact data; create EmployeeType, EmployeeStatus and five remaining proposed support catalogs. Owner-approved disposable schema renumbering only; do not run Hibernate update over existing populated tables.

## FDN-DATA-001 — Add approved UserType, Language, Location and MeasurementUnit

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner approved the four retained entities and requested implementation on 2026-11-24. Baseline 6a82e3ea1b99fd27060153ac26316ab6d4a116ee, CI #23 passed. Dependencies: accepted ORG-DATA-001; existing Identity and Reference persistence models. Implement the previously proposed dependency-order table allocation on fresh disposable schemas only. The owner excluded Company; preserve Organization and Infra byte-for-byte, AuthorizationScope including companyId, and all existing business behavior. No optional preferredLanguageId on User (not essential and not separately selected), QuantityType, conversions, external identity, seed data, APIs, migrations or deployment.

- UserType: immutable unique code (HUMAN, SERVICE only), trilingual names; User requires explicit same-module userType, no implicit/default persisted type or classification-based authorization.
- Language: unique lowercase two-letter language code shape, trilingual names, nativeName, rightToLeft, active default true/retire. No claim of ISO catalog membership or translation completeness.
- MeasurementUnit: immutable unique uppercase stable code, trilingual names, case-sensitive symbol text (not a unique key), active default true/retire. No unit conversion, quantity classification, reference-condition handling or solver qualification.
- Location: trilingual name (at least one), finite WGS84 latitude/longitude in decimal degrees within physical range, optional finite elevation in metres above mean sea level (survey datum/engineering qualification deferred), optional same-module Locality. No route geometry or extra entity. Optional elevation is not defaulted to zero.
- Localized names follow current 120-character convention with at least one nonblank name; UserType and MeasurementUnit codes max 64; Language code length 2, nativeName max 120; MeasurementUnit symbol max 40, case preserved.
- Identity table order: existing 01–06 unchanged, UserType 07, User 08, Permission 09, LocalCredential 10, LoginSession 11, AuthenticationEvent 12, GroupMembership 13, RolePermission 14, UserRoleGrant 15, GroupRoleGrant 16, RefreshToken 17. User adds F_07 userType (FK_03), preserve F_00–F_06 and A_01.
- Reference table order: Country 01 unchanged, Language 02, MeasurementUnit 03, State 04, District 05, Locality 06, Location 07. Update dependent named constraints, raw SQL tests and inventory. No table ID duplication. AGENTS examples updated only to reflect User's new table number; governance rules unchanged. The prior proposal explicitly presented this renumbering; owner-approved implementation is interpreted to include the published dependency order, not existing-data migration.

## FDN-SCOPE-001 — Represent company boundary by a classified organization unit

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner decision 2026-11-24: Company is a specific case of OrganizationUnit; companyId becomes organizationUnitId and the referenced unit must have the company organization-unit type. Baseline dd84c80bf3d07c068464b1509cef229d6774b2b0, [Build #25](https://github.com/CHOUABBIA-AMINE/HyFloAPI/actions/runs/45483175416) success. Depends on accepted FDN-DATA-001 and ORG-DATA-001. Supersedes the previously unresolved companyId decision only; no separate Company or OrganizationUnitKind entity, no new tables or columns, no renumbering.

- Keep F_06 required, immutable positive scalar ID in both embedded grant scopes; rename Java field/getter and all factory parameters to organizationUnitId. Existing GLOBAL/REGION/PIPELINE/INFRASTRUCTURE meanings remain company-bounded; F_05 and F_07 unchanged.
- OrganizationUnitType.COMPANY_CODE = "COMPANY" is the stable code, not a translated name or arbitrary root-unit assumption. No new enum or seed data. Organization owns the exact case-sensitive type check of a persisted unit (query returns type code, not entity). Missing and non-company units rejected identically; positive ID required.
- Admit internal OrganizationUnitScopeService.requireCompanyUnit(long) (Organization owner; Identity application consumer; no public HTTP/API; caller must already be authorized to request the grant). Identity AuthorizationScopeService.validateCompanyBoundary(AuthorizationScope) returns the same structurally valid scope after owner validation. Both grant construction paths in the synthetic integration scenario use it. Pure domain scope factories still validate shape only. No claims of universal write interception, database cross-module FK, effective-date/status eligibility, resource ownership or request-time authorization; those require separate selected protected grant/evaluation use cases. The owner check is deliberately type-only as requested.
- Preserve existing table layouts, historical role-grant behavior, audit actors and temporal scope. No cross-module entity/repository imports by Identity, no external identity, migration, seed or deployment.

## IDN-01 — Identity initialization and trusted administrator bootstrap (candidate)

Status: BLOCKED — workflow-driven Identity pilot is selected for design by the owner; the next proposed package combines initial reference/catalog initialization and the first trusted administrator. Baseline 435f3ea5e48eb4bfabbbe7d1a718f231c27b8d9b, Build #30 passed. Dependencies: accepted FDN-SVC-001, FDN-REPO-001, FDN-SCOPE-001, Organization company eligibility. No implementation authorization/envelope yet. This is a recorded decision gate, not an executable task. The wider 42-operation / 12-package pilot inventory remains a design proposal until individually registered; no other module selected.

Verified blocker: UserRoleGrant.grantedBy is a required FK to an already persisted User; ordinary grant actors must derive from authenticated context. The empty Identity database has no authenticated granting administrator. The existing OrganizationUnitScopeService validates only the COMPANY type, not current company eligibility. Identity cannot create an Organization unit by itself, and no Organization catalog or company rows have been seeded. A first grant must not use a fabricated authenticated actor, a nullable FK, an ordinary self-grant, a permanent HTTP bootstrap endpoint or a default superuser bypass.

Recommended single owner decision for IDN-01:
- Explicit, opt-in, one-time non-HTTP bootstrap command executed by a trusted deployment operator on a fresh development Identity schema. No automatic startup privilege creation. Credentials supplied externally, hashed using a supported password encoder; no plaintext persistence or logging. Never overwrite existing credentials or re-grant authority on repeated execution.
- Require an already provisioned COMPANY-classified OrganizationUnit, validated through an Organization-owned contract for type, active status and effective dates; missing/ineligible company fails closed. No automatic Company entity or Organization provisioning inside Identity; use synthetic Organization fixtures in tests. Provisioning of an initial company for a clean environment remains an explicit owner prerequisite.
- Use a dedicated persisted UserType SERVICE bootstrap attribution account, permanently DISABLED, no LocalCredential, no LoginSession and no role grants. This is a proposed one-time trusted initialization attribution exception to normal authenticated-actor governance, not a new UserType, a login-capable account, or a claim that a human was authenticated. Capture the trusted operator reference and initialization provenance in the initial grant reason without secrets; attribute the first human admin grant to this technical account. Ordinary later grant paths must still derive an authenticated actor and must not use this exception.
- Initialize LOCAL, HUMAN/SERVICE, PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED/DISABLED and only explicitly approved action/resource/permission definitions. Proposed initial role IDENTITY_ADMIN is restricted to the supplied company and approved Identity administrative capabilities; no wildcard or engineering permission, no platform-wide GLOBAL, and no implicit privilege from UserType. The initial exact capability manifest must be included in the implementation envelope before code; definitions for later workflows do not authorize HTTP operations until enforced by their selected functional tasks.
- Atomically provision the named HUMAN admin, encoded credential, valid activation, approved role/permission associations and first company-scoped grant. Prove concurrency safety and atomic rollback on failures; concurrent/repeated initialization cannot create another initial admin or restore revoked authority. No login endpoint, JWT, generic CRUD, seed on ordinary startup, new model fields/tables, production migration or deployment as part of this candidate.
- Required acceptance: real disposable MySQL positive initialization/readback, secrets not exposed, technical actor cannot authenticate, exact company and explicit permission scope, missing/invalid company rejection, disabled-by-default invocation, invalid inputs and rollback, duplicate/repeated/concurrent initialization behavior, preservation of constraints and existing tests. Future functional service changes must preserve bean discovery and same-module repository ownership while replacing only superseded empty-skeleton assertions. Exact implementation paths, commit message and approved permission catalog must be registered after the decision and before edits.

No source edits under this decision gate. The next executable package requires explicit approval of the bootstrap attribution exception and a completed registered implementation envelope; no subsequent task may be advanced automatically.

### IDN-01 approved implementation — 2026-11-25

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner explicitly approved the one-time disabled SERVICE attribution account proposal. Baseline 5923bbf58628cf8a7af5293ebb9ec357cd8c08ab; [Build #31](https://github.com/CHOUABBIA-AMINE/HyFloAPI/actions/runs/45572722929) success. Depends on accepted Foundation baseline and a pre-provisioned eligible Organization company. This entry resolves the preceding decision gate without changing normal authenticated actor rules. No other task selected.

Implementation contract:
- Explicit sole argument `--identity-bootstrap` dispatches from HyFloApi to a non-web Spring context, invokes IdentityBootstrapCommand and closes the context; ordinary startup never invokes initialization. No HTTP or automatic runner. Read only external environment HYFLO_BOOTSTRAP_COMPANY_ID (positive Long), HYFLO_BOOTSTRAP_USERNAME, HYFLO_BOOTSTRAP_EMAIL, HYFLO_BOOTSTRAP_PASSWORD, HYFLO_BOOTSTRAP_OPERATOR (trusted operator reference, bounded safe characters). Reject missing/invalid inputs, reserved bootstrap identity and additional command arguments. Password minimum 15 characters, maximum 72 UTF-8 bytes for bcrypt, no blank secret; shared platform PasswordEncoder bean uses Spring delegating bcrypt encoding. No default or password in arguments/logs/result. Command failure returns nonzero through a sanitized exception. Development schema only, existing DB configuration/profile preserved.
- Organization owner contract requireEligibleCompanyUnit(long, LocalDate) checks exact COMPANY code, active type, ACTIVE unit status and half-open date validity at UTC initialization date. Query the owner entity with fetched type/status and pessimistic read lock; no JPA entity returned to Identity. Keep original classification-only contract unchanged. No Organization seed or creation.
- All initialization in one Identity application transaction via module-owned repositories with only explicit count/save operations; reject any existing independent root (IdentityProvider, UserStatus, UserType, Action, ResourceType, Role, Group), which also excludes dependent Identity rows via existing FKs. Insert the unique LOCAL provider row first, before other identity writes; its unique constraint and transactional insert serialize competing initialization attempts even with different company IDs. Losing attempts fail, no retry/upsert; any failure rolls back the whole initialization. Existing data or repeated invocation fails without mutation; manual out-of-band destructive DB changes not supported.
- Create LOCAL, PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED/DISABLED, HUMAN/SERVICE. Create reserved username hyflo-bootstrap with reserved non-routable email hyflo-bootstrap@hyflo.invalid, SERVICE, DISABLED, no credential/session/grant. Create supplied HUMAN admin with no employee assumption, encoded credential and valid activation. Initial grant is GLOBAL (supplied company only), with reason containing IDN-01 and externally supplied trusted operator reference (not independent identity proof); grantedBy is the persisted disabled attribution account. No user schema changes or generic actor exception for other workflows.
- Initial explicit capability manifest (resource.action codes, each distinct ResourceType/Action pair) chosen conservatively from the pilot: user.read, user.create, user.activate, user.suspend, user.reactivate, user.disable, credential.initialize, credential.reset, login-session.revoke, role.read, permission.read, user-role-grant.read, user-role-grant.assign, user-role-grant.revoke, identity-catalog.read. Seed only these 15 definitions, their distinct action/resource codes and one IDENTITY_ADMIN role with exactly these associations. Stable technical codes, English display labels and trilingual UserType names. These are reserved definitions for the approved Identity pilot, not implemented HTTP capabilities. No wildcard, global role/group mutations, engineering permissions, security history across companies or platform superuser. Later catalog additions require selected functional tasks and actual scope enforcement. 
- No login/refresh/JWT endpoint, no runtime administrative authorization, no API DTO/controller, no new entity/table/column, no schema migration, no change to existing entity services/structural assertions. Existing temporary Spring Security baseline remains only for existing smoke tests; not an authorization bypass or usable bootstrap administrator login.

## IDN-02 — Local authentication and stateless access-token enforcement

Status: PLANNED — registered next eligible functional package of the owner-selected Identity pilot following IDN-01 acceptance. Baseline b67ac5b2840ad0c4b8bc9c1f37b8c1dc4824ec47; Build #33 passed. Dependencies: IDN-01, FDN-SCOPE-001, FDN-REPO-001, FDN-SVC-001. No implementation in this acceptance/registration commit. Next selects this package after refreshing exact-SHA CI; no subsequent package automatically selected.

Workflow coverage: IDN-W12 local login, IDN-W13 access-token validation, IDN-W40 successful/failed login evidence. Provide minimal own authenticated identity read as prerequisite to later profile operations; IDN-W14/15 refresh rotation/logout and all administrative/scoped authorization operations remain later work. Business owner Identity; User status/provider eligibility, LocalCredential verification, LoginSession validity, initial RefreshToken digest and AuthenticationEvent are existing model responsibilities; no new entities/fields/tables. Platform security owns cryptography, servlet filter chain, bounded technical throttling and safe HTTP security errors. No external provider, HTTP session or generic CRUD.

Contract and invariants:
- POST /hyflo/api/v1/auth/login accepts validated JSON LoginRequest (username and password only; existing username normalization; nonblank bounded input, bcrypt 72 UTF-8 byte maximum). Success 200 TokenResponse with accessToken, tokenType Bearer, accessExpiresAt, opaque refreshToken, sessionExpiresAt; no-store/no-cache. GET /hyflo/api/v1/me returns CurrentIdentityResponse (own user ID, username, session ID; no grants, credential, cross-user or company data). No generic User endpoint; explicit mapper from safe immutable application result records, never entity serialization.
- Resolve LOCAL provider by exact stable code, user by normalized username/provider, current status/provider and own credential. Only ACTIVE eligible HUMAN/SERVICE accounts with valid credential and enabled LOCAL provider; reserved disabled bootstrap actor cannot authenticate. Use existing IdentityPasswordEncoder explicitly, reject unsupported/malformed credential hashes, no plaintext/noop fallback; use a precomputed dummy bcrypt hash for unresolved/ineligible accounts and generic login rejection to reduce enumeration. No credentials, JWTs, refresh values/digests or sensitive request bodies in logs. Malformed HTTP input gets safe 400; valid but rejected credentials get indistinguishable 401; throttling gets generic 429. Server generates correlation ID; no trusting caller actor/source headers.
- Application login transaction: lock matching User row (pessimistic write) and read its credential/provider/status inside the transaction; use same owner lock ordering for future credential/account lifecycle changes. On success persist one LoginSession, one cryptographically random opaque refresh token digest (SHA-256 lowercase hex of 32 random bytes encoded as base64url without padding), LOGIN_SUCCEEDED event; sign access JWT, commit before response. Rejected known/unknown user with existing LOCAL provider records LOGIN_FAILED with optional user ID and returns a rejected application outcome (not an exception that rolls back evidence). Before LOCAL initialization or when audit cannot be persisted, deny without inventing a provider/event; operational readiness failure distinct from success, no sensitive details. Successful login failure injection must rollback all three writes and return no tokens. No rotation/refresh endpoint yet; issued refresh tokens unusable until IDN-04. Session revocation and expiry are enforced now even though the revocation API remains later.
- Signed access JWT HS256 with explicitly selected algorithm, external base64-encoded key of at least 32 random bytes, strict issuer/audience hyflo-api, access token type, subject positive user ID, session ID, jti, iat/exp. Validate signature, algorithm, required claims, issuer/audience, expiry and positive identifiers; token expiration bounded by session expiry. Reject invalid/missing/expired/wrong-session tokens, no client-supplied privilege claims, no role name/permission claim authorization. Defaults proposed for development pilot: access 10 min, absolute session 8 h, configurable positive bounded durations (access max 15 min, session max 24 h, access no longer than session), no hardcoded signing key. Servlet startup fails closed if key/config absent or invalid; non-web bootstrap context must not depend on JWT key or servlet-only components.
- SessionAuthenticationService is the explicit Identity owner contract to platform.security: after signature validation, accept immutable user/session IDs and current server time, return immutable own principal IDs/username only if session is active and belongs to ACTIVE user with enabled LOCAL provider; no JPA model or private repository crosses boundary. Evaluate on every protected request; DB errors fail closed. No claims of permission evaluation, cross-company/resource coverage or stale grant authorization. No cached authorization.
- Replace temporary in-memory HTTP authentication (remove its configuration and disable actual Boot user-details auto-configuration using verified dependency/source) with a servlet-only stateless SecurityFilterChain: no Basic/form/remember-me or cookie auth, no HTTP session or saved request; CSRF disabled only for bearer-only JSON API, CORS not opened globally. Permit POST login and health GET; require live bearer identity for GET /v1/me and existing enabled development OpenAPI/Swagger read surfaces; deny every other route, including engineering/administrative endpoints, until explicitly admitted by IDN-03/later workflows. No authenticated blanket business access. Filter must run once inside chain, not additionally auto-registered as servlet filter. Authentication failures use 401, authenticated disallowed routes 403; safe ProblemDetail with stable code/correlation ID where available, English fallback until localization selected. Keep actuator exposure config; non-health endpoints not admitted.
- Before expensive credential work, apply bounded thread-safe single-process login limiter (global, direct remote IP, normalized username independent limits; no forwarded-header trust). Configurable fixed window default 60s, defaults global 120, IP 20, username 10 attempts; bounded bucket capacity and expired-entry eviction, fail closed if saturated, 429 and Retry-After. Only syntactically valid attempts reach expensive auth; do not expose identity existence. This is development single-instance rate limiting, not distributed attack protection or permanent account lock. No schema change.
- Reuse existing entity services unchanged; implement coherent AuthenticationService workflow and SessionAuthenticationService rather than adding generic repository CRUD. Add only explicit same-owner lookup/save methods to six repositories, preserving IDN-01 count/save methods. Wire servlet-only token/HTTP collaborators without making non-web initialization require signing configuration. Existing 37 entity service skeleton assertions remain valid; new orchestration classes do not replace entity-specific services. Existing password format and bootstrap behavior preserved.
- Update the previous temporary-auth smoke test deliberately: anonymous and wrong/old valid Basic credentials must be 401; replace positive Basic proof with successful persistent login -> authenticated OpenAPI read in the new real-MySQL HTTP integration scenario. Do not make docs public, weaken health/scope checks, or suppress tests. Test-only JWT key is synthetic. No new dependencies, migrations, POM/workflow changes, or deployment.

## IDN-03 — Effective permission and company-scoped authorization

Status: PLANNED — registered next eligible package of the selected Identity pilot, baseline 6fab6dea44f621b7e57d01a66723a91fec5a2e05; CI #35 passed. Dependencies: IDN-01, IDN-02, FDN-SCOPE-001 and accepted Foundation model/repository/service baseline. Next selects this package following exact-SHA CI refresh. No implementation or later task selection in this registration.

Workflow coverage: IDN-W36/37 direct effective permission resolution and protected company-level authorization, with a minimal own-scope entitlement consultation (part of IDN-W39) and one actual protected read of the existing initialized Identity catalog. Identity owns grants, roles, permission definitions, account/session eligibility and authorization decisions. Organization owns authoritative company eligibility; platform.security owns authenticated principal and route enforcement. No general authorization-check HTTP oracle, no cross-user search, no grant/role/group changes or seeded permissions.

Scope and semantics:
- Reuse UserRoleGrant, Role, RolePermission, Permission, Action, ResourceType and AuthorizationScope. A GLOBAL grant is company-wide, never application-wide. Match exact requested positive company OrganizationUnit ID; accept only exact GLOBAL with null scopeReferenceId. The request's companyId is the requested boundary, not evidence of access or company membership. The recipient's optional employeeId, username, actor role name, or token claims never confer scope. Only current eligible authenticated user/session with enabled LOCAL provider can receive an authorization decision. Resolve actor from the authenticated CurrentIdentity principal (not an HTTP actor parameter, header or JWT privilege claim) and revalidate using the existing SessionAuthenticationService inside the authorization read transaction. Internal calls accept that trusted principal only, not an arbitrary caller-supplied user ID.
- Use server-selected Instant per decision, UTC company date, half-open validity and revocation rules via existing isActiveAt, enabled roles and actual RolePermission links. Query only the user's grants for the requested company; prefilter candidates by time/role/scope as useful and enforce exact case-sensitive shape and enabled role in Java (MySQL collation cannot define security equality). Resolve exact stable permission code and action/resource pair, validate the code equals resourceType.code + "." + action.code, deduplicate and deterministically sort. An exact permission match is required; no prefix, wildcard, implicit role hierarchy or implicit privilege from grant actor. Never consider grantedBy account eligibility a condition of an otherwise valid grant; approved bootstrap attribution stays disabled.
- IDN-03 implements direct grants only. The earlier pilot package IDN-10 owns group membership/inherited role evaluation; GroupRoleGrant and GroupMembership do not confer runtime authority yet. REGION, PIPELINE, INFRASTRUCTURE grants remain stored but cannot grant access here; even matching scalar IDs or a GLOBAL grant cannot authorize a real engineering resource without an authoritative resource-owner coverage contract. No invented target owner or permissive fallback. User account company ownership and multi-company administrative mutation semantics remain unresolved for IDN-05/08; this task does not infer them or expose account management.
- Introduce Organization-owned isEligibleCompanyUnit(long, LocalDate) read-only boolean contract for Identity authorization. Fetch the unit, type and status through its own repository, check exact COMPANY code, active type, ACTIVE status and half-open validity. No entity crosses module boundary. Preserve the classification-only and initialization-locking contracts. Invoke the new contract only after a matching effective direct permission candidate exists (for a specific protected operation, after the exact required permission matches), so users without authority cannot use Organization lookups to probe protected company existence. Unknown, non-company, inactive, expired, future, retired-type and unauthorized company are indistinguishable at HTTP denial. No company name or other protected metadata returned.
- EffectiveAuthorizationService exposes a trusted internal read decision/require contract for company-level capabilities plus own effective company permission codes. A trusted owner selects the required exact permission; no endpoint accepts arbitrary permission checks or user IDs. Evaluate session, grants, role-permission links and Organization eligibility in one explicit fresh read transaction (repeatable-read consistent snapshot for standalone reads against the current single MySQL database). No persistent/cached entitlements, JWT authorization claims or cross-request permission reuse. For the actual catalog read, authorization and catalog fetch must occur in the same transaction; do not disclose data, counts, existence or query the catalog before the gate. A later protected write must specify its own locking/serialization and revocation race contract and re-evaluate authority in that write transaction; IDN-03 read proof is not authorization atomicity for future mutations. No long-lived decision or historical authorization replay.
- GET /hyflo/api/v1/me/permissions?companyId=<positive-id> returns CompanyPermissionsResponse {companyId, permissionCodes} sorted unique exact definitions only for the requesting principal within an eligible company with at least one effective direct permission. It is an entitlement view, not a promise that every listed operation is implemented or a downstream resource is authorized. Missing/no valid permission or unavailable company returns generic 403 without existence metadata. No caller-supplied actor. GET /hyflo/api/v1/identity/user-statuses?companyId=<positive-id> requires exact identity-catalog.read on that eligible company and returns a deterministic code-sorted list of UserStatusResponse {id, code, displayName}, existing persisted catalog values only. The catalog is shared reference data; company-scoped authority authorizes this specific read, not ownership of the shared catalog or permission to edit it. No new classification seeds or REST CRUD.
- Add explicit AuthorizationController and mapper; use existing safe AuthenticationProblems and response conventions (400 INVALID_REQUEST for missing/nonpositive/malformed companyId, 401 for absent/invalid live authentication, 403 ACCESS_DENIED for missing grant/permission or ineligible company, 503 IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE for failed persistence/decision). No internal exception or existence disclosure, no-store for protected responses. Admitted GET routes only, preserving all previous login/health/me/docs behavior and deny-all remainder. No business access solely because the route is authenticated; application must gate read before accessing catalog. No blanket /identity/** rule, no roles in SecurityContext or permission claim changes. No generic CRUD.
- Explicit repository additions only: candidate direct grants by user/company and role permission associations for selected role IDs, user status ordered read, Organization eligibility fetch. No generic CrudRepository expansion, model/table changes, new dependencies, migrations, configuration changes or existing scaffold-method test relaxation. Existing authentication, bootstrap and scope contracts remain intact. EffectiveAuthorizationService and IdentityCatalogService are focused workflow orchestration, not replacements for entity-specific skeletons.
- Do not duplicate Audit ownership or extend AuthenticationEvent (login events only). This read stage supplies safe request-local decisions and correlation in errors; no durable AuthorizationDecision or audit persistence is claimed. Audit/evidence for sensitive mutations and security-history access must be registered with their respective packages. No actual production operation, industrial resource authority or deployment.

Implementation commit: feat(identity): enforce effective company-scoped direct permissions

## IDN-04 — Refresh rotation and own-session revocation

Status: IN PROGRESS — selected by owner Next (IDN-004 denotes the existing IDN-04 candidate), 2026-11-25. Base 456a934a378cbee2e14b5299693f9e509a649051; Build #37 passed. Dependencies: accepted IDN-01/02/03. One task, no split; reuse existing LoginSession, RefreshToken, AuthenticationEvent and stateless access JWT. No new tables, columns, schema changes, migration, dependencies, external identity, admin session management, or production deployment.

Registration commit: docs(identity): accept direct authorization and register session lifecycle

Implementation commit: feat(identity): rotate refresh tokens and revoke own sessions

Semantics and scope:
- POST /hyflo/api/v1/auth/refresh with explicit JSON RefreshRequest {refreshToken}, no access token required (client must omit Authorization when access expired; existing filter behavior unchanged). Accept only canonical unpadded base64url encoding of 32 bytes, no raw value in logs/DTO toString, persist SHA-256 hex only. Apply existing bounded global and peer-IP authentication limiter budget (shared with login) before database lookup; do not retain token/digest as a limiter key. Preserve login username bucket semantics and existing safe 429 error code for compatibility. Unknown valid-shaped token is generic 401, invalid shape 400; no owner or session disclosure.
- Rotation within one READ_COMMITTED write transaction: use scalar digest lookup for session ID and scalar session owner ID, then pessimistic User -> LoginSession -> RefreshToken locks in that order, check exact digest, owner/session consistency, live eligible LOCAL account and half-open session interval with server time sampled after locks. Never trust a caller actor/session/company ID. Consume old token, insert successor digest, issue signed access JWT with original session expiry (no sliding extension), append REFRESH_SUCCEEDED evidence and return existing TokenResponse only after transaction commits. Keep old consumed rows for replay detection; existing access JWTs remain valid until expiry or session revocation. No new session on rotation. Concurrent login uses the existing User lock; future account lifecycle tasks must follow the same lock ordering; no claim of global atomicity with unimplemented external account/provider changes.
- Strict replay policy: when a consumed known token is presented for a currently active eligible session, revoke that entire session and append one REFRESH_REPLAY event in the same transaction, then return generic 401 as a normal rejected outcome (not an exception that rolls back revocation). Concurrent duplicate refresh yields at most one success; replay may immediately invalidate the successful response's session. Clients must serialize refresh; no grace window or idempotent token reissue. Inactive/expired/unknown requests create no new records, no replay event duplication. Any write/signing/evidence failure rolls back and returns safe 503; no partial credentials or success response.
- POST /hyflo/api/v1/auth/logout with valid live bearer and no target selector. Require CurrentIdentity from authenticated context, recheck same owner and current eligible session under User -> LoginSession locks, revoke current session and append existing LOGOUT event atomically. Return 204/no-store; subsequent bearer and all session refresh tokens denied. An already revoked/expired session or invalid bearer returns 401; other sessions remain unaffected. No cross-user or all-session revocation.
- Keep AuthenticationEvent existing physical schema and stable login codes; add technical REFRESH_SUCCEEDED and REFRESH_REPLAY event constants requiring attributable user; use server correlation IDs, no token data in evidence. Preserve existing DTOs, JWT claim structure, authorization rules, repository/service skeletons and deny-all remainder. No generic CRUD or new permissions. No CSRF change, cookies or server HTTP session. No assumption of distributed rate limiting or production TLS.
- Update architecture/module documentation with the actual implemented boundary only, no claim of global write authorization.

Unit: canonical token shape/redacted DTO, global/IP shared budget, existing username limit preserved, finite event codes. Actual HTTP: initial login -> rotate without bearer -> same user/session, distinct successor/digest, unchanged session expiry, original token consumed timestamp, one success event; access JWT bounded by session expiry, no plaintext/digest leakage beyond intended opaque response, no cookies/cache. Unknown/malformed/missing, invalid bearer, suspended/disabled user/provider, expired/revoked sessions denied; no unintended record changes. Concurrent refresh/logout: serialized parent mutation, no active session/successful post-revocation refresh, no duplicate successor or logout event, previously issued access denied after commit. Independent session survives. Explicit rollback trigger on evidence insert: rotation preserves unconsumed old token and no successor, replay failure preserves session and no replay event, logout failure preserves live session, all return 503; retry after trigger removal succeeds or denies according to policy. Exercise own-session logout and no arbitrary user/session target; no new authorization surface.
- Exact-SHA HyFloAPI Build mandatory with no skips/failures, real MySQL and retained test/SBOM/JAR evidence. Source inspection is not runtime proof. Optional security scan separate. No acceptance recorded before CI.

Owner-review design choices: strict session revocation on replay including duplicate concurrent requests; fixed original session lifetime, existing access JWT validity until expiry/revocation, authenticated current-session logout only, shared login/refresh global and IP budget with existing error code. These are bounded implementation decisions, not previously explicit owner security policy or industrial acceptance.

### IDN-05 admission review — 2026-11-25

Disposition: BLOCKED — candidate for protected user registration and lifecycle administration; not selected for executable source changes. IDN-04 prerequisite accepted above. IDN-03 explicitly leaves account ownership and multi-company administrative mutations unresolved. Current User (T_10_01_08) has optional employeeId and no accountable company identity; its status and credential apply to the entire account, not one company. AuthorizationScope on UserRoleGrant identifies the scope of permission, not ownership of the recipient account. EmployeeAssignment is effective-dated and optional from the account perspective; it cannot establish permanent account administration authority, particularly for SERVICE accounts and users without an employee link. EffectiveAuthorizationService.requireCompanyPermission is an admitted company read gate only, not a write-authorization contract. No currently registered policy establishes who may globally suspend, disable, reactivate or reset a multi-company account.

Proposed owner decision (NOT APPROVED): one explicit accountable company per User, referencing the existing COMPANY-classified OrganizationUnit by scalar ID; separate company administration ownership from multi-company scoped access grants. Company-local administrators can administer only accounts owned by their company and only with exact operation permission; employment association remains optional, and grant revocation in a different company does not disable the global account. Other companies may manage their own scoped grants only under separately selected IDN-08 authority. Alternative: shared global identities administered by a distinct explicitly authorized central identity authority, with company admins restricted to their own grants. Do not infer global authority from GLOBAL scope, IDENTITY_ADMIN role name, an employee assignment, or a target user's existing grants.

Before executable registration, the owner must choose the account ownership policy; then specify the creation/lifecycle authority, safe bootstrap and disposable schema mapping if required, account-wide credential/session consequences, actor evidence, and authorization-at-write locking/revalidation contract. No new entity/column, route, permission, source/test change, or later task is authorized by this acceptance checkpoint. No production-data migration assumed. This checkpoint is documentation only.

Registration commit: docs(identity): register company-owned account lifecycle
Implementation commit: feat(identity): enforce company-owned account administration

- User has required immutable positive owningOrganizationUnitId (scalar existing COMPANY-classified OrganizationUnit; no cross-module entity/FK or Company entity). Add F_05 after employeeId/profilePictureFileId, renumber existing provider/status/type to F_06/F_07/F_08 and provider/username UK_01 accordingly, preserve table/other fields, version and named FK identities. Fresh disposable schema only; no migration or reuse of existing schema. Bootstrap attribution and administrator both receive the validated company. Existing raw model fixtures use explicit positive synthetic IDs; runtime fixtures use persisted company references. Company ownership is not operational authorization; no change to multi-company grants, access claims or login. No ownership transfer or employee linkage operation here.
- Admit only GET /v1/identity/users (bounded keyset page afterId default 0, max 100 rows), GET /v1/identity/users/{userId}, POST /v1/identity/users, and POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/activate, /suspend, /reactivate, /disable, each with explicit companyId selector. No generic patch/delete, credentials, external provider, grant management or public event/history route. Read via exact user.read; create via user.create; transitions via exact respective user.activate/suspend/reactivate/disable. Creation takes username/email/userTypeCode/reason only, LOCAL provider, PENDING, no credential/employee/profile field or implicit grant. Validate reserved bootstrap identity and supported persisted catalog exact codes. Account list/detail include only owned accounts, never reserved bootstrap actor. Authorized read returns explicit id, username, email, companyId, userTypeCode, userStatusCode. No secret or grants. Creation returns 201 with same DTO; transitions 200. Missing/foreign targets indistinguishable 403; self-administration mutations denied, no last-admin policy claim. Global normalized provider/username and email uniqueness remains DB-enforced; conflicting create is generic 409 without field/other account/company metadata. No prelookup across companies. Invalid input 400, missing live auth 401, forbidden 403, rejected transition 409, DB/evidence failure 503, no-store. No caller-supplied actor, owner in body, event time or target status.
- Exact authorized write in READ_COMMITTED transaction: match SecurityContext principal, pessimistically lock actor User and its LoginSession and enabled LOCAL provider, revalidate live eligibility, lock direct candidate grants/roles and actual role-permission links/permission/action/resource rows (pessimistic read joins), match exact GLOBAL company scope, finite permissions, current grant half-open validity and enabled role, then call Organization-owned locked eligibility contract (existing initialization lock query extended by purpose-specific service method). Only after gate query target by both ID and owningOrganizationUnitId with pessimistic write lock; no target lookup before permission and company eligibility. Recheck authority/time after waiting for target lock and immediately before operation. Keep authority rows locked until commit; future grant/role/company mutations must honor same DB row locking and no bulk bypass. Possible reciprocal actor/target deadlock fails closed with 503 and rollback (no blind retry); no global serializability claim. Reads retain IDN-03 repeatable-read snapshot and company gate. Suspension/disable revoke all target unrevoked sessions while holding target User lock, including all companies, so reactivation cannot resurrect tokens. Preserve User -> LoginSession order used by login/refresh/logout. Activation strictly PENDING, reactivation strictly SUSPENDED; both require existing local credential and enabled provider. Disable from PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED, terminal; repeated disable rejected. Credential provisioning/reset remains separately selected work; synthetic fixture credential proves transition. No last-admin or central authority bypass.
- Identity owns immutable UserLifecycleEvent business transition evidence (not general Audit or AuthenticationEvent): new next dependency-order table T_10_01_18, F_00 id, F_01 eventCode, F_02 occurredAt, F_03 correlationId, F_04 reason, F_05 previousStatusCode (null only creation), F_06 resultingStatusCode, F_07 owningOrganizationUnitId, F_08 target User FK_01, F_09 performedBy User FK_02. Required actor from authenticated context, target ownership snapshot, no password/token data; finite registered transitions; append in same transaction as change and session revocation. No generic audit subsystem or evidence export. New entity-specific repository/service functional; Foundation count 38, preserve all earlier structural checks except replacing its temporary skeleton-method expectation with concrete service contract. Historical allocation check: existing inventory ends at 17; historical pre-correction tree 6763b5d12655fe02404af2a43d1807bb90d2e8fb has no UserLifecycleEvent. No changes to AuthenticationEvent or bootstrap attribution exception.
- Owner-review implementation choices: immutable ownership, LOCAL-only registration without optional employee link at this stage; exclude bootstrap actor and self-administration; bounded keyset read; existing global uniqueness; mandatory reason, separate lifecycle evidence, all-session revocation on suspension/disable; no automatic activation, no credential endpoints, no owner transfer. Development only.

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner Next, 2026-11-25. Baseline 46385c66c7807cb9c68cb3f82ddcefbc201eb081; accepted IDN-01 through IDN-05 and Build #41. One whole task. No schema changes, migration, new entity, reference values or permissions.

Registration commit: docs(identity): accept account administration and register credentials
Implementation commit: feat(identity): initialize and reset company-owned local credentials

- POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/credential/initialize and /credential/reset, each explicit companyId selector, request {password, reason}, return 204/no-store with no credential, token, hash or account data. No public credential reads or generic update, self-service password change/recovery, external identity, email delivery, automatic activation, password history, forced rotation at first login or administrator session-revocation endpoint. Owner-review development design: authorized administrator supplies initial/replacement password directly; separate secure delivery/forced-change workflow is not implemented or claimed. No secrets in logs/DTO toString or evidence; reason is nonsecret mandatory text, reject direct inclusion of the submitted password. Input uses existing bootstrap password policy: at least 15 Unicode code points, nonblank, maximum 72 UTF-8 bytes (bcrypt); no trim/normalization. Use the existing IdentityPasswordEncoder, persist only supported tagged bcrypt. Retain exact current login supported hash format and share its recognition in LocalCredential; no alternate algorithm or noop fallback. Reset rejects a password equal to current supported hash's original password; malformed legacy hash can be replaced, not authenticated. No historic password reuse check.
- Reuse UserAdministrationService.lockAuthority within READ_COMMITTED mutation (same live actor/session/provider/grant/role/permission/company locks). Check exact operation permission before target query, lock target User by id AND owning company; deny self/reserved bootstrap/foreign/unknown identically, recheck after locks and before evidence. LOCAL enabled, exact status required. Initialize only PENDING with no credential, unique User FK and User lock serialize duplicate requests; existing credential is conflict, no implicit reset. Reset only PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED with existing credential, never DISABLED, update same LocalCredential row via domain operation, retain ownership and nondecreasing passwordChangedAt. No change to target status or grants. Revoke every unrevoked target session on reset, all companies, using existing User -> LoginSession ordering shared by login/refresh/logout; no old session resurrection. No implicit reset on initialization; no arbitrary actor/target company or timestamps in body.
- Append CREDENTIAL_INITIALIZED / CREDENTIAL_RESET finite technical event codes to existing UserLifecycleEvent using same previous/resulting status (PENDING for init, PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED for reset). Actor/target/company/reason/correlation/time only; never password/hash in event. Persist credential, revocation and evidence atomically, rollback on any failure; no secrets in response. Generic 400 invalid input, 401 live authentication, 403 forbidden, 409 wrong lifecycle/credential state or same password, 503 unavailable. Preserve IDN-05 reads/transitions and default deny. Separate next candidate IDN-07 administrative session revocation; not authorized here.

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner Next, 2026-11-25. Baseline df7d488e8f94b148b2dd96b123818eec0ec6f579; accepted IDN-01 through IDN-06, Build #42 success. One whole task. No entity, schema, seed, dependency or new permission.

Registration commit: docs(identity): accept credential management and register session administration
Implementation commit: feat(identity): revoke company-owned account sessions administratively

- POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/sessions/revoke with explicit companyId selector and existing UserLifecycleRequest {reason}; 204/no-store on success, no session count, IDs, tokens or other data returned. Owner-review bounded design: revoke all currently live, unrevoked sessions of a target account at the serialized decision time, including all its operating companies; no per-session selection, session list/read, own-all-session endpoint, grant/credential/status changes or general audit export. Existing own-session logout remains unchanged. No implicit session-read capability from login-session.revoke. A subsequent successful login can create a new session; account suspension or disable is a distinct operation.
- Within READ_COMMITTED transaction reuse UserAdministrationService.lockAuthority and exact login-session.revoke, with authenticated live actor, direct company grant and locked company eligibility. Deny self, reserved bootstrap, foreign/unknown account uniformly; target lookup only after gate and by id AND owning company under pessimistic User lock. Recheck authority and target LOCAL provider after lock. Use existing findUnrevokedForUpdate in stable id order under target User lock; recheck authorization after obtaining session locks and sample server time, truncated to micros; revoke only rows still unrevoked and half-open live at that time, leave expired/future/already revoked sessions unchanged. No restriction on target status beyond valid supported persisted status; emergency revocation may clean up an inconsistent live session on an inactive account without reactivating it. If no eligible session, generic 409, no mutation or evidence. Concurrent duplicate all-session revoke at most one success if no intervening login; subsequent sessions are not retroactively revoked. Revalidate authority before evidence; rollback on persistence/lock failure, fail closed 503. Preserve User -> LoginSession ordering with login/refresh/logout and prior account mutations. Account-level evidence SESSIONS_REVOKED in existing UserLifecycleEvent, unchanged previous/resulting status (PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED/DISABLED), actor/target/company/reason/correlation/time only; no per-session audit precision claim. Event and all revocations atomic. Mandatory nonblank reason max 255, no caller actor/time. Generic 400/401/403/409/503 using existing safe errors.
- No new read or broad route admission. Next candidate IDN-08 direct role grant administration subject to separate registration and security policy review.

### IDN-08 admission review — 2026-11-25

Disposition: BLOCKED pending explicit owner delegation policy; candidate direct role grant administration, not selected for executable source changes. IDN-07 prerequisite accepted above. IDN-01 seeded only IDENTITY_ADMIN, with all 15 pilot permission definitions including credential.reset, user-role-grant.assign and user-role-grant.revoke. Current Role is a reusable global permission bundle (no company owner or delegation metadata); UserRoleGrant stores recipient, role, company/resource scope, half-open validity, reason, granting/revoking actor and revokedAt. Existing effective authorization accepts direct active enabled GLOBAL company grants and does not condition recipient entitlement on the ongoing authority of grantedBy (IDN-03; preserve bootstrap attribution and no implicit cascading revocation). The existing locked write gate proves an actor's exact operation permission but does not prove entitlement to delegate a role or its validity period. Approved Option A separates single account-owning company from multi-company grant scope; it does not explicitly authorize replication of the sole seeded administrator role or define delegation of account-control privileges to users controlled by another company. Without a decision, implementing assign could grant durable administrative authority, or an owner of a foreign-company account could indirectly exercise the company's delegated administrator rights by resetting that account's credential. No implicit global role hierarchy or central authority is established. Do not treat permission presence or role name as delegation approval.

Proposed owner decision (NOT APPROVED): bounded company-local administrator delegation for this development pilot. Permit assignment of an existing enabled role only with exact user-role-grant.assign, current direct GLOBAL authority for the same role in the same eligible company, valid exact role-permission links and a requested finite grant interval fully covered by that actor's own active grant. No self-assignment/revocation; no implicit scope widening, group or resource-scoped assignment; no role/permission creation or change. Permit administrative role delegation (including IDENTITY_ADMIN) only to a target whose accountable company is the grant company; deny administrative privilege delegation to foreign-owned accounts, including roles with account/credential/session/grant administration capabilities. Ordinary non-administrative roles may be assigned to a foreign-owned recipient by explicit ID under the same role/interval restriction, with no disclosure of its profile or grants in other companies. An eligible company admin may read/revoke grants only in the selected company with exact user-role-grant.read/revoke; administrative revocation authority over existing grants and protection of the initial bootstrap grant must be explicitly specified at executable registration. Grant changes must preserve actor attribution, target locking and lock-based write authority, duplicate/overlap and time checks; no cascading revoke, no global account mutation. Existing seeded administrator remains the trusted starting authority; this decision does not create additional companies' initial administrators or solve general last-admin recovery. Alternative: reserve administrative role assignment to an explicitly admitted trusted provisioning path; runtime assign only non-administrative roles under the same restrictions, requiring ordinary role provisioning before meaningful runtime assignment on the current seed.

Before executable registration, choose whether company-local administrators may delegate the existing administrative role or whether that role remains restricted to trusted provisioning. Exact endpoint/file scope, administrative capability boundary, grant interval and bootstrap protection, grant overlap/revocation/evidence semantics, catalog visibility and acceptance must be registered after the decision. No new entity, permission, seed, route, code/test change or later task authorized by this checkpoint.

Checkpoint commit: docs(identity): accept session administration and record grant delegation gate

## IDN-08 — Direct company role grant administration

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner explicitly approved Option A with proposed restrictions, 2026-11-25. Baseline e9597c06fdfd4d3e1be71e9decfaa67b28609a93, [Build #45 attempt 1](https://github.com/CHOUABBIA-AMINE/HyFloAPI/actions/runs/45620926296) success, verify job 139228520689, 19 unit and 13 real MySQL integration tests passed without failures/errors/skips; mandatory artifacts retained, optional scan skipped. IDN-01 through IDN-07 accepted. This registration supersedes only IDN-08 admission blocker. One whole task.

Registration commit: docs(identity): register company-local direct role delegation
Implementation commit: feat(identity): administer bounded company direct role grants

- Reuse T_10_01_15 UserRoleGrant, existing role/permission models and embedded AuthorizationScope. No schema/catalog/permission changes. GET and POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/role-grants, POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/role-grants/{grantId}/revoke; explicit companyId. Exact user-role-grant.read/assign/revoke respectively. GET bounded keyset afterId default 0, max 100, selected company GLOBAL rows including history; missing or foreign-with-no-grant returns empty, no account lookup/profile, no total count. POST assignment takes roleId, mandatory finite ISO Instant validTo (microsecond-exact, server-selected validFrom at decision, strictly future, max MySQL year 9999), mandatory nonblank reason max255. No caller actor, scope, validFrom or revoke timestamp. Return 201 explicit grant projection; revoke 204/no-store, no request body required. Projection only grant ID, recipient ID, role ID/code, companyId, scopeType, validity/revocation times, original reason and granting/revoking actor IDs. No other-company profile/permissions. Existing grant records themselves provide immutable assignment and revocation actor/time evidence; original reason not overwritten; no separate revocation reason/correlation stored (schema-free limitation). No generic CRUD, resource-scoped read/write, role/permission catalog mutation, group grants, cascading revoke or implicit account mutation.
- Reuse existing locked write gate, READ_COMMITTED, exact selected company operation authority first; lock target User, no self/unknown/reserved bootstrap, target LOCAL provider enabled, PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED for assignment, disabled denied. For assignment require actor's own currently active direct GLOBAL grant of exact same enabled role covering entire requested interval (single grant; no union, no extension beyond validTo/revokedAt). Lock actual RolePermission links and reject empty/malformed permission bundles. Same-company administrative delegation permitted. Foreign-owned recipient allowed only if every role permission is in the explicit non-administrative pilot catalog-read allowlist identity-catalog.read, role.read, permission.read; unknown or any other permission (including user.read) conservatively treated as privileged. No invented engineering permission classification or role-name based bypass. Future role permission edits must preserve this invariant and lock protocol; not authorized here. Recheck exact authority and coverage at decision time after target and candidate locks. Reject overlaps against existing same target/role/GLOBAL/company effective intervals (including future grants; revoked historical interval ends at min(validTo,revokedAt)). Use target User lock to serialize inserts/revocations; no unique constraint replacing historical intervals. No self-grants. Revoke only exact selected company GLOBAL grant and selected target under locks; no self, reserved attribution account, or grant originally issued by hyflo-bootstrap (initial authority protected); may cancel future or currently live grants, already revoked/expired conflict, no dependency cascade; no requirement to hold revoked role, exact revoke permission suffices. Recheck before change, preserve existing grant reason and grant actor; use micros, no account status/session/credential changes; existing same JWT immediately sees committed effective grant change. Missing/wrong-company/wrong-role authority denied 403, invalid 400, conflict 409, unavailable 503. No full last-administrator or trusted recovery guarantee. 
- Owner-review bounded choices: immediate start only, finite requested expiry, conservative foreign permission classification, GLOBAL-only administration, protected bootstrap-issued grant, existing grant evidence rather than new schema. No new roles seeded, synthetic ordinary catalog role in tests only. Next candidate IDN-09 role/permission catalog consultation; not selected here.

### All 42 elementary workflows

P0 = essential security functionality; P1 = subsequent administration/usability. Coverage labels are documentation observations of the bounded registered scopes, not new task statuses. Accepted means only the indicated registered development scope, Partial means the original workflow is broader than accepted coverage, Open means no applicable completed executable workflow was established, and CI-passed/acceptance pending does not mean COMPLETED. No production or operational acceptance is implied.

**Inventory check:** 42 workflows (22 P0, 20 P1), grouped by the source's seven business areas: initialization 2; account management 9; authentication/session management 8; permissions/roles 7; groups 5; authorization 8; traceability 3. Each workflow appears once in the primary design allocation. W04 is allocated to original IDN-06 because the actual credential workflow is registered there; W17 is allocated to original session lifecycle IDN-04 but live IDN-07 covers only administrative account-wide revocation. W38 is allocated to original direct grant package with group history still open. W41 is tracked under the final integrated gate for inventory counting only: evidence remains mandatory in each individual package. IDN-W36/W37/W39 span later inherited and real resource authorization; never infer owner coverage from scope shape alone. Local-only authentication, deny-by-default, actor/temporal traceability, explicit company boundaries, no generic CRUD or arbitrary capability creation, and no industrial or production acceptance remain in force.

This reference table does not register the missing executable envelopes or advance the next task. The preceding register remains authoritative for exact scope, dependencies, acceptance, messages and next selection.

## IDN-09 — Role and permission catalog consultation

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner Next, 2026-11-25. Baseline f91e2318171e5bfb9dddf81f65a678837630f422, Build #47 success, IDN-01–08 accepted. Implements the consultation portion of original design package IDN-07 / IDN-W22; live IDN-09 is not the original group package. Dependencies: IDN-01 existing definitions and IDN-03 company read authority; IDN-08 accepted delegation prerequisite. One whole task; no mutation of role bundles, permission definitions or grants.

Registration commit: docs(identity): accept direct grants and register catalog consultation
Implementation commit: feat(identity): expose authorized role and permission catalogs

- Admit only GET /v1/identity/roles, GET /v1/identity/permissions, GET /v1/identity/roles/{roleId}/permissions with required positive companyId selector. Each is a shared catalog definition read (not company-owned role definitions, membership, grants, actors, account data or effective entitlements). Exact role.read for role list; permission.read for permission list; both for role bundle consultation. No assumption that holding a role confers read authority or that reading a role permits delegation. Reuse existing live-principal/direct GLOBAL company gate in the same read-only REPEATABLE_READ transaction as the catalog query; check required authority before any catalog fetch, no cross-company grant lookup or target existence disclosure. No new seed, permission, entity, schema, localization, write route, group grant or resource-owner coverage.
- Fixed maximum 100 rows per query, ascending positive ID keyset afterId (default 0); no totals or offset. Role projection id/code/displayName/enabled includes retired definitions as metadata, not authorization. Permission projection id/code/displayName/actionCode/resourceTypeCode/validDefinition; validDefinition uses existing exact resource.action validation, not route availability or effective access. Include malformed persisted definitions only as non-valid metadata; never grant access from this projection. Role bundle query returns only linked definitions, including historical retired role definitions, empty when no links or unknown role; both permissions required before query. Explicit DTOs/mapper, no entity serialization. Generic safe 400 for invalid selector/cursor/role ID, 401/403/503 via existing read adapter, no-store. No generic CRUD or arbitrary capability creation.

## IDN-10 — Own local password change (IDN-W18)

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner Next, 2026-11-25; baseline 02484521b6020e151e183d16412df944b4c52da9, Build #49 success. Next sequential live ID, not the original design IDN-10 (group grants/inherited authorization); that design package remains open. Completes W18 residual of original IDN-06, with W41 self-service evidence. Dependencies: accepted IDN-02 authentication, IDN-04 session lifecycle, IDN-06 credential replacement, IDN-09 acceptance sequencing. No group or role changes. One whole task.

Registration commit: docs(identity): accept catalogs and register own password change
Implementation commit: feat(identity): change own password and revoke sessions

- POST /v1/auth/password/change (under /hyflo/api) bearer-only JSON currentPassword/newPassword, no user/company/session/actor selector, no token response. Target exclusively authenticated CurrentIdentity, eligible ACTIVE LOCAL HUMAN or SERVICE; never reserved account. Require live principal and session revalidated within transaction. Both passwords nonblank, max 72 UTF-8 bytes; new password also minimum 15 Unicode code points and same existing DTO 72 UTF-16 unit bound, no trim/normalization. Exact tagged bcrypt only; verify current secret, reject same replacement, store only supported encoded hash; no password history/recovery/forced reset. Reuse shared bounded global/peer/normalized authenticated username attempt limiter (counts valid-shape requests, existing 429/Retry-After); no password/token keys, no distributed limiter claim. Invalid shape/policy 400, invalid live identity/current secret/same password generic 401, persistence/lock/evidence failure 503, success 204 no-store. No client-supplied attribution or passwords in logs/DTO diagnostics. Existing error contracts preserved.
- READ_COMMITTED application transaction, verify SecurityContext equality; lock User first, then current LoginSession and credential owned by User; existing login/refresh/admin reset share User lock. Recheck exact current session ownership, principal username, eligibility and active interval at decision after credential verification and locking all unrevoked account sessions. Replace same LocalCredential via named domain method at server microsecond time, revoke all unrevoked sessions including current (future stored sessions revoked at their start, expired rows may be marked revoked consistent with IDN-06); no other account, role, status or grant changes. Old access and refresh credentials rejected on subsequent requests; fresh login required. Concurrent duplicate changes serialized, at most one succeeds with old password/session; login/refresh ordering cannot resurrect pre-change sessions. No promise of canceling in-flight authorized reads.
- Reuse AuthenticationEvent T_10_01_12 for PASSWORD_CHANGED and PASSWORD_CHANGE_FAILED (both fit current 24-character eventCode; attributable User/provider, correlation, server time; no new schema). Success event atomic with hash/session changes; wrong/missing/malformed current stored credential or same password on live eligible account records failure before returning generic rejection; unauthenticated/invalid shape/throttled requests do not append password events. Evidence failure rolls back and fails closed. No general audit export or session listing. Conservative owner-review choice: revoke every session including current and require new login; no silent token renewal. Existing administrative reset unaffected.

## IDN-11 — Own-session consultation and selective revocation (IDN-W16/W17)

Status: IN PROGRESS — owner Next, 2026-11-25; baseline a8eda1dd4c14bbc2335b461136df95ec7c80dcdf, Build #50 success. Next sequential live ID, not the original design IDN-11 profile/search/history package. Original IDN-04 residual W16 and W17; W41 evidence incremental. Dependencies: accepted IDN-02, IDN-04, IDN-07 and IDN-10. One coherent task.

Registration commit: docs(identity): accept password change and register own sessions
Implementation commit: feat(identity): consult and selectively revoke own sessions

- GET /v1/auth/sessions?afterId=0 returns only the authenticated account's currently active unrevoked sessions, ascending ID, max 100, no totals, ID cursor default 0, no caller account/company selector. Explicit DTO sessionId, startedAt, expiresAt, current; no refresh secrets/digests, IP/device inference, credentials or unrelated account data. Reuse exact SecurityContext principal, current-session ownership/eligibility and temporal revalidation within same read-only REPEATABLE_READ transaction before listing. Snapshot semantics, not promise of live state after response. Invalid cursor 400, invalid current identity 401, failure 503, no-store.
- POST /v1/auth/sessions/{sessionId}/revoke, no required body, authenticated self-service only, no admin permission or target-account selector. READ_COMMITTED, User lock then current session lock, validate identity before own-account-scoped target lock, recheck live current and target at microsecond decision, revoke exactly another active unrevoked owned session. Current session target, foreign/missing/expired/future/revoked target uniformly 403; invalid ID 400; stale current identity 401; success 204 no-store; failure 503. Use existing LoginSession.revoke; same User lock serializes login/refresh/logout/password change/admin reset/revoke; concurrent duplicate target at most one success. Do not change credentials/status/grants or other sessions. Target's access and refresh denied on subsequent requests; current logout remains existing endpoint.
- Precise W41 evidence: extend existing AuthenticationEvent T_10_01_12 with nullable immutable same-module LoginSession association named loginSession, F_06, named FK T_10_01_12_FK_03 referencing T_10_01_11.F_00, no cascade. Existing constructors/events remain compatible with null; SESSION_REVOKED requires target session owned by same user and revokedAt equal to event occurredAt, domain-enforced, all other event codes keep null. Actor user/provider, target session, server time and correlation recorded atomically with revocation. No current/actor session reference or general audit history. This is a fresh disposable development schema addition, not migration, no new table or seed. Preserve existing physical identifiers; update schema inventory and MySQL column/FK assertions. Owner-review design choice: explicit target association rather than ambiguous account-only evidence; retain current session and deny selecting it, use logout for current session.

This is a proposed eligibility-at-link-time rule, not a claim that work placement establishes legal employment ownership. Preserve immutable account owner and do not implicitly add/revoke grants, migrate ownership, suspend accounts or unlink upon transfer; if the owner requires continuous eligibility, define the transfer/offboarding contract before implementation. Recommend explicit administrative link/unlink with distinct persisted actor, reason/correlation, before/after employee IDs and dedicated permission; decide whether reassociation is permitted. Maintain module boundaries; scalar cross-owner reference, database uniqueness and physical FK mechanism must be explicitly addressed with fresh-schema proof (no foreign JPA entity coupling or unauthorized Flyway adoption). New permission provisioning must be explicitly admitted under current seed governance.

## IDN-13 — Company-owned employee association (IDN-W11)

Status: PLANNED — approved and registered for next execution, not implemented. Dependencies: accepted ORG-DATA-001, IDN-03, IDN-05, IDN-12. Original IDN-05 W11 residual; W41 incremental evidence. One coherent task. Registration commit: `docs(identity): register approved employee association workflow`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): manage assignment-validated employee association`.

- Optional single Employee association for HUMAN account only, globally at most one account per employee, including disabled accounts until explicitly unlinked. SERVICE remains unlinked. Current employee company derives from the one effective assignment and nearest COMPANY ancestor, no duplicated Employee or EmployeeAssignment company field. Organization-owned internal boolean contract in EmployeeAssignmentService, not a cross-module entity/repository return: in caller write transaction lock employee, current/future assignment rows under existing employee serialization, lock employee status and every traversed unit/type/status, validate exact ACTIVE statuses, active unit types, half-open employment/assignment/unit dates in UTC, single effective assignment, positive IDs, no cycles/ambiguous or missing path. Lock via existing OrganizationUnitRepository.findForInitialization; extend employee repository with explicit locked eligibility fetch, and assignment repository as required. Validate selected company and final decision date after all locks; fail closed for missing, foreign or ineligible employee. No employee/person mutation or new Organization HTTP surface.
- Account owner is immutable; association is eligibility-at-link-time only. Subsequent transfer, retirement or offboarding does not automatically mutate account, association, grants, status, credentials or sessions. No privileges derived from employeeId. Unlink remains possible after employee transfer or retirement, as cleanup is authorized by existing account owner. Replacement requires explicit unlink then link; no silent overwrite. Link permits PENDING/ACTIVE/SUSPENDED HUMAN, unlink also permits DISABLED HUMAN. Deny reserved/self targets, non-LOCAL/ineligible provider, foreign/missing targets uniformly. Dedicated user-employee.read and user-employee.associate permissions (engineering choice: distinct read and mutation). Add only these two capabilities to existing trusted fresh-schema bootstrap IDENTITY_ADMIN; no runtime automatic grants, no upgrade of populated identity schemas. Preserve foreign-delegation allowlist.
- GET /v1/identity/users/{userId}/employee?companyId=... requires exact user-employee.read in existing read-only REPEATABLE_READ gate; return explicit userId and nullable employeeId only, no employee personal data or global search. POST same path /link (positive employeeId and required nonblank <=255 reason) and /unlink (existing reason DTO) require exact user-employee.associate, 204/no-store; validate authority before target/employee existence, READ_COMMITTED and existing locked write authority, account lock then Organization employee/assignment/hierarchy locks, recheck live authority before change. Duplicate/already-linked or unlinked state generic 409, malformed 400, unauthenticated 401, denied 403, unexpected persistence/evidence failure 503. Concurrent links to same employee serialize via employee lock and DB unique constraint; no raw conflict metadata. Existing W06 DTO unchanged.
- Preserve User.F_03 nullable scalar; domain constructor and named link/unlink methods enforce HUMAN-only, positive reference and state transitions. Named nullable UNIQUE T_10_01_08_UK_03(F_03) and physical FK T_10_01_08_FK_04(F_03 -> T_10_02_09.F_00), no cascade. No foreign JPA entity imports. Concrete mechanism: Identity infrastructure UserEmployeeReferenceConstraint, eager component dependent on entityManagerFactory, JdbcTemplate information_schema exact column/target/constraint verification, fail startup on invalid/missing required constraint. Only explicit dev/update on empty disposable User table or test/create-drop may create missing named FK after Hibernate DDL; verify named UNIQUE and FK afterward, no automatic repair of populated or validate schemas, no Flyway. Test/create-drop teardown drops only this externally managed FK before EMF closes; no default/dev teardown DDL. Test fresh schema and default validation fail closed. This is an explicitly scoped fresh-schema integration mechanism, not production migration.
- Immutable UserEmployeeAssociationEvent T_10_01_19 (next unused table in live Identity inventory; historical roadmap and retrieved prior architecture revisions contain no allocation; verify historical source before implementation): F_00 id, F_01 eventCode (LINKED/UNLINKED), F_02 occurredAt, F_03 correlationId, F_04 reason, F_05 owningOrganizationUnitId, F_06 previousEmployeeId, F_07 resultingEmployeeId, F_08 user FK_01, F_09 performedBy FK_02. Employee IDs are historical scalar snapshots, not mutable associations. Require distinct persisted actor/target, exact before/after shape and agreement with target's resulting employeeId; immutable evidence appended via owner event service in same transaction. No edits to existing lifecycle event identifiers. Add one repository and one corresponding service; no general audit export.
