### W20/W21/W22 controlled capability registration — selection gate, 2026-11-25

Status: BLOCKED (candidate IDN-15, not a registered executable task). Source assessment at d08eaa65051c7e1ea3f3bfc01509845870595837: Action and ResourceType have globally shared immutable code and displayName, Permission is a globally unique resource/action pair. IdentityBootstrapService admits the fixed 17 permission definitions through explicit trusted initialization; requireFreshIdentity rejects populated Identity roots. HyFloApi and IdentityBootstrapCommand already support an explicit sole-argument non-web trusted command with operator reference. EffectiveAuthorizationService.validPermission establishes structural code equality, not runtime capability support. Existing GLOBAL grants mean company-wide; no platform-wide administrative authority exists. IDN-08 foreign-recipient catalog-only grant invariant remains binding.

Recommended owner decision (proposal only): admit a separately selected, explicit non-HTTP capability-registration command, using a reviewed source-controlled manifest of exact implemented resource/action pairs and canonical labels. Reconcile additively and idempotently on initialized disposable development schemas, serialize registration transactions on a stable existing Identity root, validate exact code and pair equality independent of collation, fail closed on drift/ambiguity, record immutable operator reference, reason, timestamp, manifest identifier/digest and inserted definitions atomically. Operator reference is attribution supplied by the trusted execution environment, not authenticated human identity proof. No arbitrary caller-supplied permission codes or executable handlers, automatic startup seed, modification/deletion of existing codes, implicit role bundle change, automatic grants or production migration. Current 17 implemented definitions can form the initial reviewed manifest; future entries need explicit scope and proof of supported operations. No invented future permissions merely to demonstrate growth; use synthetic fixtures for growth and rollback tests. Define label change/retirement separately if required. Fresh bootstrap should consume the same reviewed manifest without silently extending the approved initial administrator bundle. Role administration remains separate: recommend defining explicit platform-wide authority for shared role definitions, or an owner-approved company-owned role redesign, before role mutation; never infer platform authority from company GLOBAL grants.

Owner approval required for the non-HTTP trusted registration authority and its additive scope before assigning an executable task ID and exact file envelope. No source/schema/seed changes selected. Only append acceptance and this decision gate to docs/ROADMAP.md. Commit: `docs(identity): accept definition consultation and record registration gate`.


### Owner direction — HTTP-only first-use setup, 2026-11-25

The owner explicitly supersedes the earlier approval of a non-HTTP Identity bootstrap: "No i want it http", following the first-use browser setup proposal, then "Next". Preserve IDN-01's historical acceptance and downstream accepted tasks; its non-web entry mechanism is now selected for replacement, not a failed historical acceptance. A newly installed development instance must be initializable through HTTP from the browser without a separate application, CLI bootstrap, direct SQL or manual company-row seeding. Normal technical deployment (database, application, JWT signing material and installation secret configuration) remains external. This direction does not select deployment or HyFloWEB implementation.

Baseline main c4662e5c65d78780c447b359b8b6bbf1e1912d7e, tree a732e50731f2f3881eecd5cca0ba7e5ca287c274, parent d08eaa65051c7e1ea3f3bfc01509845870595837; [HyFloAPI Build](https://github.com/CHOUABBIA-AMINE/HyFloAPI/actions/runs/45677505881) completed/success at this exact SHA. Current source: HyFloApi dispatches the sole --identity-bootstrap argument to a non-web context; IdentityBootstrapCommand reads five environment variables; IdentityBootstrapService validates an already-existing eligible COMPANY and initializes the fixed 17 permissions and first administrator in one transaction. No browser company creation or setup endpoint is implemented. Organization owns OrganizationUnit, OrganizationUnitType and OrganizationUnitStatus; the current repositories have no company creation operation. IDN-15 remains BLOCKED, not selected: its separate ongoing shared-capability registration authority and role-administration policy are unresolved. Do not interpret HTTP first-use approval as platform-wide authority or approval for IDN-15's proposed non-HTTP command.

## IDN-01-HTTP — Secured HTTP first-use initialization (IDN-W01/W02 follow-up)

Status: PLANNED — owner-selected direction, registered here for the next implementation execution after exact-SHA registration CI. This is a new corrective workflow referencing accepted IDN-01, not a rewrite or split of that completed task. Dependencies: accepted Organization data/scope foundation, IDN-01/02/03 and subsequent accepted Identity security contracts through IDN-14. Registration commit: `docs(identity): register secured HTTP first-use initialization`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): replace command bootstrap with secured HTTP setup`.

- HTTP contract: under existing /hyflo/api context, GET /v1/setup/status returns only {available:boolean} (no-store); this one deliberately public readiness bit enables browser setup discovery, not user/company existence, IDs, counts or protected data. Available only when valid setup configuration and fresh Identity plus initial Organization unit/type/status roots permit setup. Nonfresh roots fail closed; do not assert successful initialization from arbitrary existing data. POST /v1/setup/initialize accepts JSON {companyCode,companyNameAr,companyNameFr,companyNameEn,username,email,password}; at least one localized company name, existing field size/normalization and password bounds, no caller-supplied role, scope, actor or company ID. Return 204/no-store on success; safe 400 for invalid inputs, 403 for missing/incorrect secret or disabled configuration, generic 409 for nonfresh/concurrent losing initialization, 503 for unexpected storage failure; never return password, hash, secret, administrator ID or session/token. Only these exact GET/POST routes are admitted to the security allowlist; no broad setup wildcard, no other anonymous business routes, no change to existing JWT authorization. Normal login follows separately.
- Installation trust: externally supplied HYFLO_SETUP_SECRET (base64 of at least 32 random bytes) and HYFLO_SETUP_OPERATOR (existing bounded operator-reference format); absence/invalid configuration disables setup without preventing normal application startup. Require X-Hyflo-Setup-Secret header for POST, strictly bound length and compare decoded secret using constant-time equality; reject missing/duplicate/malformed header, authenticate setup authority before any protected state lookup or write. No default secret, URL/query credential, raw secret logging or persistence; do not log request bodies. Accept only JSON for mutation, never form/cookie credentials. Require trusted HTTPS and restricted installer access in nonlocal deployments; network enforcement is not claimed by application tests. Public status returns only the availability bit. Operator reference is deployment-supplied attribution, not proof of authenticated human identity.
- One atomic cross-owner transaction via a new IdentitySetupService: validate all supplied data and setup authority; require empty Identity independent roots and empty OrganizationUnit/OrganizationUnitType/OrganizationUnitStatus roots for new first-use installation; Organization-owned OrganizationSetupService creates exactly COMPANY type and ACTIVE status using existing catalog entities and one root unit with requested code/names, UTC current validFrom, null validTo and no parent. Only scalar new company ID crosses module boundary. Use named unique constraints on COMPANY/type code, ACTIVE/status code, organization code and existing LOCAL provider as durable serialization gates; force inserts before dependent use, no upsert, no retry of privilege creation. All changes including company, catalogs, disabled non-login bootstrap attribution account, encoded administrator credential and exact existing 17 permission/grant bundle commit together or roll back. Retain IdentityBootstrapService's company eligibility check and original validation, fresh-root gate and existing immutable grant actor/reason/timestamp evidence; change comment/entry admission to HTTP setup service, preserve existing IDN-01 reason prefix and record trusted operator from configuration. No arbitrary seeds, no new schema/table/column, no automatic grants to existing accounts, no automatic initialization on ordinary startup. Subsequent requests, restarts and configuration changes cannot reopen populated Identity/Organization roots; destructive database resets are outside the contract. A pre-populated or partial development schema is not adopted or repaired by setup. Organization's other existing owner contracts and downstream Identity operations are unchanged.
- Remove IdentityBootstrapCommand and the sole-argument non-web dispatch from HyFloApi; no replacement command or startup runner. Remove obsolete bootstrap environment instructions and update current documentation to distinguish historical accepted CLI from replacement implementation. Internal bootstrap application operation remains private by convention to the setup orchestration and synthetic test fixtures; never expose direct company-ID bootstrap as HTTP. Preserve existing test fixtures that exercise company eligibility and downstream workflows through the application service where necessary; end-to-end first-use proof must use actual HTTP on a fresh MySQL schema.

### IDN-15 selection gate reconciliation — 2026-11-25

Status: BLOCKED — candidate only, not a registered executable task. The earlier recommendation of a non-HTTP capability-registration command conflicts with the owner's revised HTTP-first product direction if applied to subsequent browser operations. IDN-01-HTTP acceptance does not authorize ongoing global capability or role administration. On the accepted SHA, Action, ResourceType and Permission remain shared definitions; IdentityBootstrapService provisions the fixed 17 permissions only on empty roots; IdentitySetupService rejects populated roots; EffectiveAuthorizationService resolves GLOBAL permissions within the selected company only. No platform-wide authority exists. Do not reopen setup, reuse the installation secret, infer global authority from IDENTITY_ADMIN/company GLOBAL grants, or add CLI/SQL workarounds.

Recommended owner decision (proposal, not selected): an explicit protected HTTP capability reconciliation operation against a reviewed source-controlled manifest, additively and idempotently registering only implemented capability definitions. For this narrow operation, authorize a live authenticated active LOCAL HUMAN operator explicitly designated by immutable user ID in external deployment configuration (disabled when absent), independent of company grants; revalidate live session/account and designation before mutation, record persisted actor, reason, timestamp, correlation, manifest identifier/digest and inserted definitions atomically, serialize on the existing Identity root and fail closed on drift. No arbitrary code input, no implicit grants or role bundle changes, no reuse of setup credential, no automatic startup mutation. This would be a narrowly bounded platform maintenance authority, not general platform administrator privileges. Alternative: separately design a persistent platform-scoped authorization model and its initial provisioning before selecting reconciliation. Shared role definition administration remains a separate decision; preserve IDN-08 foreign-recipient delegation restrictions. Owner must explicitly select an authority model before registering IDN-15's exact envelope, including evidence storage and fresh-schema proof as applicable.

Only docs/ROADMAP.md is changed by this acceptance/gate record. Commit: `docs(identity): accept HTTP setup and record capability authority gate`. No new implementation, schema, seed, privilege or subsequent stage selected. Stop after publication and confirmation of applicable exact-SHA CI.


### IDN-15 owner decision — persistent platform authority (Option B), 2026-11-25

The owner explicitly selected Option B (persistent, database-managed platform-level authorization over HTTP), superseding the proposed configuration-designated operator and historical non-HTTP capability-registration proposal. Baseline main e8f1b4162ca0637db83b7e5252902bcefa5d979e, parent d348c3a56452533e66e905f5e35950a9e80d4b5f, tree f1db00cded7c8b3fd5bf8a49cdc4b0e3f94ee6de, [Build #62](https://github.com/CHOUABBIA-AMINE/HyFloAPI/actions/runs/45684637442) completed/success. This resolves the authority-model choice only; it does not yet authorize a particular initial privileged account, seed bundle or schema alteration. Keep the accepted HTTP setup and existing 17 company-scoped permissions intact until a separately registered amendment.

Source basis: AuthorizationScope requires a positive organizationUnitId for all existing types; GLOBAL is company-wide, not platform-wide. UserRoleGrant and GroupRoleGrant embed this contract; EffectiveAuthorizationService.matchesCompany and UserRoleGrantRepository company queries explicitly select GLOBAL and a company. Role is a globally shared bundle with no authority-level discriminator; Permission is a globally shared action/resource pair. Every User has immutable owningOrganizationUnitId; UserAdministrationService admits company-owned account lifecycle/credential/session mutations. SessionAuthenticationService resolves live LOCAL HUMAN or SERVICE sessions. A platform grant on an account that remains subject to company-admin credential resets or lifecycle mutations would create an indirect cross-company privilege escalation or denial-of-service path. No platform permission resolution or provisioning exists.

Platform authorization prerequisite (design proposal, not an executable envelope):
- Identity owns persistent platform authority. Preserve company GLOBAL and existing regional/resource scope semantics; no fake company ID, wildcard, nullable-company shortcut in existing grants, or inference from role name. Recommend dedicated platform-role grants with temporal validity, persisted grant/revoke actor and reasons, and a distinct authority-level role classification (or separate platform role bundle model if justified by the detailed mapping). Enforce disjoint platform/company grant and permission eligibility in both write and effective-resolution paths; existing company routes cannot create, delegate, revoke, or use platform grants. Keep IDN-08 foreign-recipient restriction intact. Platform grants are not automatic access to company engineering/OT data.
- Use live authenticated ACTIVE LOCAL HUMAN identities, explicit exact platform permission codes, deny-by-default, read/write transaction separation, locked live account/session/provider/grant/role/permission rechecks and durable mutation evidence. No group-derived platform authority, SERVICE platform authority, wildcard permissions, self-escalation, configuration-selected standing privileged account, CLI, direct SQL, or setup-secret reuse after initialization. Never put authoritative permissions solely in JWTs.
- Recommend first platform administrator as a distinct HUMAN account created atomically via the existing secured first-use HTTP setup, with separate credentials and only explicitly approved platform permissions; the company administrator retains its separate existing 17-permission grant. Both accounts may retain the required Organization owner reference without treating it as platform authority. A persisted platform-managed account boundary must prevent ordinary company administrators from resetting its credentials, changing its lifecycle, revoking its sessions, linking its employee, or otherwise taking control, including after platform grant expiry/revocation; enforce this in all applicable current write paths and future group/grant paths. No automatic platform privilege for existing company administrators. Alternative initial provisioning: one initial HUMAN account with explicitly separate platform and company grants and the same protected account boundary; this concentrates privilege and is less desirable. No silent conversion of populated schemas; existing accepted disposable-schema fixtures and fresh-schema setup remain the development basis. Require explicit policy for recovery/last effective platform administrator before admitting platform administration or revocation workflows.
- First implementation prerequisite must establish persistent platform authorization and explicitly authorized first-use provisioning with actual HTTP/MySQL tests, exact permission isolation (including same-JWT revocation and company-admin credential/lifecycle bypass denial), atomic setup rollback/concurrency, constraints and actor attribution. Physical model/table allocation, lock ordering, precise permissions, account management/recovery policy, complete file allowlist and commit message must be registered before source changes. Do not pre-create unused administration endpoints. Capability reconciliation remains a separate subsequent task: reviewed source manifest, additive/idempotent registration, no implicit grant/bundle changes, persisted actor/reason/time/correlation/manifest digest, atomic rollback and drift rejection.
- Remaining material owner decision: select first-use platform administrator provisioning (separate dedicated HUMAN account recommended, or same initial HUMAN account with two independent grants) and approve its protected account boundary and no automatic upgrade of existing company administrators. This is a privilege-provisioning decision, not a request to choose Option B again. Exact initial platform permissions and recovery/last-admin mutation policy must be bounded by the chosen prerequisite scope; role/grant administration beyond it remains separate.

IDN-15 remains BLOCKED on the platform-authority prerequisite; the model direction is APPROVED, initial privileged-account policy and implementation envelope are not yet approved/registered. No new live task ID, table identifier, schema, seed, source change or deployment authorized here. Only docs/ROADMAP.md append-only. Commit: `docs(identity): approve persistent platform authority direction`.


### Owner direction — runtime-configurable Identity and authorization, 2026-11-25

The owner challenged treating runtime administrative configuration as fixed implementation decisions and then selected Next. Preserve approved Option B (persistent platform authorization over HTTP), but supersede the prior requirement for another owner decision about two initial administrator accounts. HyFlo supplies mechanisms, supported application capability definitions and immutable safety/security invariants; authorized installers and runtime administrators configure users, role definitions, groups, permission associations, grant assignments and supported scopes. Do not require owner approval of each ordinary configuration choice or impose a fixed customer role hierarchy or number of administrators. A minimal trusted initial authority is a security prerequisite, not a customer role template. Stable implemented capability codes cannot be invented by a catalog edit, and a role label is never an authorization gate. Preserve existing accepted company behavior and history; no implicit privilege escalation or populated-schema conversion. Baseline 479e4c070a79131dbc86f055e5b2650066a7556b, tree 2b86dae89d8c454e7f393a60493a1f91cf2f06b6, parent e8f1b4162ca0637db83b7e5252902bcefa5d979e, [Build #63](https://github.com/CHOUABBIA-AMINE/HyFloAPI/actions/runs/45689338285) completed/success. IDN-15 remains a separate candidate behind the persistent authority prerequisite.

## IDN-PLATFORM-01 — Persistent platform authority and runtime role creation

Status: PLANNED — registered for next execution after registration CI. Dependencies: accepted IDN-01-HTTP, IDN-02/03, IDN-05–08, IDN-09–14; approved Option B and runtime configuration direction. One coherent prerequisite with an actual protected read and configuration mutation, not an empty infrastructure stage or a split of IDN-15. Registration commit: `docs(identity): register runtime-configurable platform authorization`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): establish platform authorization and runtime role creation`.

- Identity owns both authorities. Preserve AuthorizationScope GLOBAL = company-wide, other existing scopes and required positive company IDs. Add immutable technical discriminator Role.platformRole (boolean F_04, default false through existing constructor, no renumbering; named platform factory or explicit constructor for true). Role code and label are data, never permission logic. A RolePermission link must agree with its role authority: reserve case-insensitive platform- resource namespace for platform capabilities; structural exact code = resource.action still required for effective permissions. Company effective read/write and delegation must explicitly reject platform roles and platform-namespaced permissions even if malformed/corrupt rows bypass domain constructors; UserRoleGrant/GroupRoleGrant reject platform role references. Existing shared catalog consultation can retain metadata visibility under current permissions; metadata never conveys platform privilege. Platform resolution accepts only structurally valid, explicitly implemented platform codes, never arbitrary database strings or a role name. No company grant confers platform authority, and platform grants confer no company/resource authority.
- New PlatformRoleGrant (T_10_01_20, next unused candidate following T_10_01_19). F_00 id; F_01 validFrom, F_02 validTo nullable, F_03 revokedAt nullable, F_04 reason, F_05 correlationId, F_06 revocationReason nullable, F_07 revocationCorrelationId nullable; F_08 user (FK_01), F_09 role (FK_02), F_10 grantedBy (FK_03), F_11 revokedBy nullable (FK_04); A_01 version last. Required positive persisted actors/recipient and platform role, HUMAN recipient, half-open validity and revocation evidence; no delete. Initial setup grant may be indefinite; no public runtime grant/revoke endpoint yet. Distinct PlatformRoleGrantRepository, corresponding PlatformRoleGrantService for initialization-only grant provisioning, and PlatformAuthorizationService for exact permission gates. Current architecture inventory and accepted fc18ccd/d08eaa6 snapshots stop at T_10_01_19; current default-branch code and commit-message searches returned no T_10_01_20/21 matches, and historical 6763b5d Identity namespace tree contains only User. Recheck applicable historical mappings before implementation, do not claim exhaustive history search. Reserve T_10_01_20 and T_10_01_21 in the architecture inventory with explicit names, fields and FKs, no Flyway or production migration.
- First-use HTTP: continue accepting the existing company and initial HUMAN account inputs; permit optional initialPlatformRoleCode and initialPlatformRoleName, validated against Role's existing lengths, nonblank bounded stable code and label, no collision with company seed role. Defaults are technical bootstrap suggestions (INITIAL_PLATFORM_ADMIN / Initial platform administrator), not mandatory role names. Create one initial HUMAN chosen by installer with existing separate company grant (17 existing permissions retained) and a separate platform grant. Only the two additional implemented platform permissions platform-role.read and platform-role.create, exact stored resource/action definitions, are seeded and linked to that initial platform role. No arbitrary initial privilege input or second account requirement. Persist initial grant actor as existing disabled SERVICE attribution user, reason with operator reference, timestamp and correlation. No existing initialized company admin is upgraded automatically. Reuse the accepted setup secret and fresh-root checks; extend the actual HTTP orchestration transaction to create both grants atomically; rollback all Organization/Identity roots on failure. Preserve the internal existing company-only initialization operation solely for synthetic accepted downstream fixtures, with no HTTP route for it; the real setup must use the platform-enabled path. No additional standing deployment credential, no re-opening setup.
- Protected account boundary without renumbering User: any account having a persisted PlatformRoleGrant history (including expired/revoked) is platform-managed for company mutation purposes. Company account lifecycle, credential initialize/reset, administrative session revoke, employee link/unlink and company grant assign/revoke must deny uniformly after authority and target User lock but before mutation; use existence lookup on the platform grant repository, never merely current effective permission. Serialize future promotion/grant changes on the same target User lock; platform grant rows are not deletable through any admitted path. Existing own password change/logout remain available. No platform account management endpoint in this prerequisite; future platform-managed account provisioning/transfer, last-admin continuity, grant assignment/revocation and recovery must enforce deterministic anti-escalation and traceability, not fixed runtime account identities. Existing account ownership reference remains distinct. No group-derived platform authority or SERVICE platform authority in this slice.
- PlatformAuthorizationService read gate: exact authenticated CurrentIdentity matching SecurityContext and live eligible ACTIVE LOCAL HUMAN session, persisted direct current platform grants to enabled platform roles, exact implemented permission and actual role-permission link; REPEATABLE_READ read with the catalog query in same transaction. Write gate MANDATORY inside READ_COMMITTED caller: lock actor User, LoginSession and LOCAL provider using existing repository locking contracts, then platform grant/role rows and role-permission/permission/action/resource rows in the existing order; recheck live identity, session and effective grant/permission after locks and before mutation/evidence. Fail closed on missing, wrong authority, retired roles, malformed codes or stale sessions. No cached grants, company selector or fallback to GLOBAL. PlatformRoleGrantRepository supplies bounded actor-specific candidate lookup and pessimistic read lock for mutation plus existence-by-recipient history lookup. No platform authority assumed from bootstrap username or role code.
- Actual HTTP runtime configuration: GET /v1/platform/roles?afterId=... requires exact platform-role.read; bounded ascending ID keyset max 100, lists shared role definitions (platformRole discriminator included), id/code/displayName/enabled, no user/grant data or counts, empty page 200. POST /v1/platform/roles requires exact platform-role.create; JSON {code,displayName,platformRole,reason}, explicit required scope flag, bounded nonblank values, stable caller-chosen code (ASCII letters/digits/underscore/hyphen, 1–64), label <=120, reason <=255; create an enabled empty role for either authority chosen by caller, return 201 with explicit role DTO and no-store. No implicit permission link, grant, privilege or handler creation. Duplicate code generic 409 including database uniqueness conflict; safe 400/401/403/503; authorize before role lookup/disclosure; exact two authenticated routes only. This delivers real runtime role definition creation; role rename/retire, permission associations and grant assignments are separate following workflows, not claimed here.
- RoleDefinitionEvent (T_10_01_21): immutable CREATED evidence, F_00 id; F_01 eventCode, F_02 occurredAt, F_03 correlationId, F_04 reason, F_05 roleCode, F_06 displayName, F_07 platformRole; F_08 role FK_01, F_09 performedBy User FK_02. Validate persisted actor, target and snapshot agreement; RoleDefinitionEventService records event in same transaction, no history export or raw secrets. PlatformRoleAdministrationService coordinates explicit list/create, write gate and audit. Reuse RoleRepository save and bounded page. No generic CRUD. Runtime role creation is independently configurable but does not establish effective authority until supported permission/grant workflows are selected.

## IDN-15 — Controlled HTTP registration of supported capabilities (W20/W21/W22)

Status: PLANNED — registered for next execution after registration CI. Supersedes the historical non-HTTP selection proposal; owner approved Option B and runtime configuration/simplicity governance. Dependencies: completed IDN-01-HTTP, IDN-03, IDN-08/09/14 and IDN-PLATFORM-01. Registration baseline c94edee83f12d0209075a20e23d7aa033f57d173; Build #67 success as recorded above. Registration commit: `docs(identity): accept platform authority and register capability reconciliation`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): reconcile supported capabilities through HTTP`.

Business mechanism and simplicity: authorized runtime administrator explicitly invokes synchronization of executable capability definitions supplied by the application, through HTTP. No customer role names, role hierarchies, permissions assigned to roles, or account assignments are prescribed by the reconciliation. Action, ResourceType, Permission remain existing Identity-owned entities. Reuse existing Spring Data/JPA repositories and authorization mechanism; one small static Java manifest, one transactional reconciliation service, one endpoint, one immutable operation event. No plugin framework, reflection scanner, configurable executable handlers, arbitrary catalog CRUD, per-definition event entities, automatic startup reconciliation, new distributed infrastructure or optimization. Runtime business role composition remains a subsequent workflow; a database entry alone cannot implement a business operation.

- Manifest: one source-controlled CapabilityManifest in Identity application/service, with immutable entries for resourceCode/actionCode/permissionCode and canonical labels; deterministic ordering and unique exact resource/action pair and permission code, lowercase ASCII code parts, existing length bounds, code = resource + "." + action. Use a stable manifest identifier and SHA-256 of UTF-8 canonical ordered entries (explicit separators, newline and fixed field order; include codes and labels). Current manifest covers exactly the existing 17 company capabilities and platform-role.read, platform-role.create, plus the new executable platform-capability.reconcile. Match the accepted bootstrap labels byte-for-byte, including "Platform role" and existing generated company labels. No unsupported future entry or caller-supplied manifest. Future code changes extend the reviewed manifest as part of their actual workflow. Fresh bootstrap consumes the same definitions, with explicit initial role permission subsets independent of manifest growth. Preserve internal synthetic company-only fixture initialization with exactly 17 definitions/links. For actual fresh HTTP setup explicitly add only platform-capability.reconcile to the initial platform role (now three platform permissions, 17 company permissions unchanged); this is necessary first-use authority for the new endpoint, not a business role template or an automatic grant on reconciliation. Existing initialized accounts are not upgraded or automatically granted the new permission. Older populated development schemas without that permission/grant cannot use this endpoint; fresh disposable schema is the supported development path, and controlled upgrade/provisioning remains a separately selected prerequisite for future non-disposable installations. Never reopen setup or use CLI/SQL/config-designated operators to bypass it.
- HTTP: POST /v1/platform/capabilities/reconcile; authenticated CurrentIdentity, required exact platform-capability.reconcile, JSON body {reason} (nonblank <=255). No company selector, operator identity, permission codes, manifest data or grant input from caller. 200/no-store response {manifestId,manifestDigest,insertedActionCodes,insertedResourceTypeCodes,insertedPermissionCodes}; arrays sorted, empty for no change. Generic 400/401/403/409 drift/503 storage errors, no internal database details or protected existence before permission. Reject unregistered paths/methods. Extend the existing platform permission allowlist with the exact new implemented code. No fallback from platform-role.create or company GLOBAL authority.
- Transaction: READ_COMMITTED; reuse live actor/session/provider/grant/role/link write gate, adding an exclusive LOCAL provider lock variant for this reconciliation operation only, acquired in the same actor -> session -> provider -> grant/role -> role-permission lock order, no shared-to-exclusive lock upgrade. Existing provider read locks and other authorization workflows stay intact. Serialize capability reconciliation across administrators on the existing LOCAL provider row, validate current authority before catalog access and again before final operation evidence; keep locks through commit. Use the existing unique code/pair constraints. For each manifest definition query by case-normalized code returning candidates (exact Java equality and canonical label required, reject ambiguous/mismatched results regardless of collation); check both permission code and resource/action ID pair, exact relation/code agreement. Reuse exact matching definitions, insert only missing manifest rows, never overwrite/delete/retire or alter role associations/grants. Drift anywhere aborts the entire transaction, including any prior inserts. Unrelated catalog rows are not deleted or adopted; no arbitrary caller additions. No need for a new serialization entity or background job. Future catalog writers must use the same serialization protocol.
- Immutable CapabilityReconciliationEvent (T_10_01_22, next candidate following T_10_01_21): F_00 id; F_01 occurredAt; F_02 correlationId (36); F_03 reason (255); F_04 manifestId (120); F_05 manifestDigest (64 lower-hex); F_06 insertedActionCodes; F_07 insertedResourceTypeCodes; F_08 insertedPermissionCodes (each non-null bounded canonical sorted newline-delimited text, empty string when none, length 8192; code validation excludes newline); F_09 performedBy User FK_01, non-null, immutable. Persist exactly one event per successful call including no-op, with persisted authenticated actor, validated bounded snapshot and UTC instant, in the same transaction as inserts. No history endpoint or extra event per definition. Recheck historical allocations before source changes: current recursive tree has no CapabilityReconciliation/Registration/Manifest files, current code and commit-message T_10_01_22 searches returned no matches, accepted historical d08eaa65/fc18ccd architecture inventories end at T_10_01_19. These are limited checks, not exhaustive history proof. Reserve exact named physical mapping in architecture inventory; no renumbering existing entities, Flyway or production migration.

## IDN-16 — Runtime role-permission management (W24/W25)

Status: PLANNED — registered for next execution after registration CI. Dependencies: accepted IDN-08, IDN-09, IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-15. Baseline 058b5fc9d7dc972a269aba4e235523d84c758737, Build #69 success above. Original design IDN-07 residual W24/W25; new sequential live IDN-16, not a split of a registered implementation. Registration commit: `docs(identity): accept capability registration and register role composition`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): manage role permissions through HTTP`.

Business mechanism: authorized platform administrators select a runtime-defined role and supported persisted permissions, inspect the catalog and existing bundle, and add/remove a single association. Role names, bundle contents and assignment choices are business data. Reuse Role, Permission, RolePermission, existing role administration service, repository and platform authorization, plus existing immutable RoleDefinitionEvent extended for permission changes. No new entity/table, role template, group or grant creation, bulk operation, generic CRUD, new service layer or optimization. Role rename/retirement (W23/W26 residual) remains a separate subsequent candidate; platform account/grant administration and recovery remain unselected.

- Exact HTTP operations under /v1/platform: GET /permissions?afterId=0 requires platform-permission.read; GET /roles/{roleId}/permissions?afterId=0 requires both platform-role.read and platform-permission.read; POST /roles/{roleId}/permissions/{permissionId}/associate requires platform-role-permission.associate; POST /roles/{roleId}/permissions/{permissionId}/remove requires platform-role-permission.remove. All bearer-only, current principal, no company selector. Read bounded ascending-ID pages max 100, positive target IDs, nonnegative cursor, same transaction as auth, explicit PlatformPermissionResponse {id,code,displayName,actionCode,resourceTypeCode,platformPermission,supported}. Include stored definitions with accurate supported flag (exact manifest code/pair and canonical labels), not only supported rows, so malformed/unsupported rows are visible for diagnosis but cannot be associated. Unknown role in bundle query returns generic 403 after authorization; empty existing bundle 200. Mutation body {reason} nonblank <=255, positive IDs, success 204/no-store, safe 400/401/403/409/503; unknown/unauthorized target generic 403, duplicate/missing link or invalid role state generic 409, no protected existence before operation permission. No caller actor or arbitrary code/handler input; no blanket route allowlist.
- Manifest version hyflo-identity-capabilities-v2 adds only those three executable codes to prior 20, retaining exact previous definitions/labels. Add three constants to PlatformAuthorizationService implemented set and explicit INITIAL_PLATFORM_PERMISSIONS (fresh HTTP setup now 17 company/6 platform, total 23). Preserve 17 company-only synthetic fixture definitions and accepted prior setup/grant behavior. Reconciliation remains additive only, no automatic grant. Existing populated installations lacking new permission links cannot use new operations; supported disposable fresh-schema development path remains, controlled upgrade/provisioning not solved here. No CLI/SQL/setup-secret bypass.
- Mutation transaction READ_COMMITTED: extend existing exclusive LOCAL provider write gate into a narrowly named catalog-write gate accepting only implemented capability-reconciliation/role-permission mutation codes; preserve existing reconciliation wrapper and noncatalog shared provider behavior. Acquire actor User -> LoginSession -> exclusive LOCAL provider -> actor grant/role/link locks, no shared-to-exclusive upgrade. All current company grant writes take shared LOCAL provider lock before reading/assigning role bundles; exclusive lock serializes composition against those writes and reconciliation. After authority, read/lock target role and selected permission/link, check invariants, recheck live exact actor authority immediately before mutation and event; hold locks through commit. No extra locking infrastructure. All future grant/group/catalog writers must participate in same lock protocol. Reuse existing unique role/permission pair, remove only the link (not definitions or grants); no blind delete.
- Domain/invariants: enabled role only, matching platform/company authority for association; permission must exactly match current manifest code, resource/action pair and canonical labels, not merely structurally valid; no arbitrary new executable codes, no implicit user/group grants. Allow removal of an existing unsupported/malformed link on eligible target role as remediation, but never use malformed definition to authorize; event must identify the actual persistent permission. To prevent indirect self-escalation, reject association if caller has any non-ended direct company grant to target role (including future start; validTo/revokedAt null or > now); reject additions to a company role with any non-ended group grant until group administration/inheritance policies exist. Preserve IDN-08: for a company role with a non-ended foreign-recipient direct grant (recipient owningOrganizationUnitId differs from grant scope organizationUnitId), association is allowed only if the entire resulting bundle contains exact supported catalog-only codes identity-catalog.read, role.read, permission.read. This covers future grants and all recorded scopes conservatively, no dependency on recipient current login/status. Use simple existence queries on UserRoleGrantRepository/GroupRoleGrantRepository, protected by exclusive provider lock; no per-recipient locks or scans needed. Keep IDN-08 existing assignment checks unchanged. Remove reduces authority; do not mutate grants or restore permissions implicitly. No global role modification via company authority.
- Interim platform privilege safety: platform roles with any non-ended direct PlatformRoleGrant (including future start) cannot have links added or removed through this slice; unassigned/ended-only platform roles can be composed. This is a conservative temporary guard against self-escalation and loss of administrative continuity, independent of role names or configured number of admins. No runtime platform grant assignment/recovery yet, so the active initial platform role's bundle is not editable through these operations. Full live platform role composition with last-effective-admin/recovery safeguards requires the subsequent platform grant/account management workflow; explicitly do not claim complete W24/W25 across assigned platform roles. Do not make a permanent reserved role or hardcoded customer role. Company role composition is available under above anti-escalation/foreign restrictions. Company administrators may lose a removed permission immediately; platform authority does not automatically supply company business data access. Restore via another appropriately authorized actor where allowed; no hidden recovery.
- Audit: reuse RoleDefinitionEvent T_10_01_21 existing immutable role snapshot, eventCode CREATED retained; add PERMISSION_ASSOCIATED and PERMISSION_REMOVED with required persistent Permission reference, append nullable F_10 permission association FK_03 to existing entity (old CREATED rows null, no renumbering or new table). Constructor/factory validates persisted actor/role/permission, event type and reason/correlation/time; CREATED requires null permission; mutation snapshots reference immutable permission code via FK and stable role fields; removal retains referenced Permission (definitions never deleted by runtime). RoleDefinitionEventService adds one explicit recordPermissionChange method with MANDATORY transaction, same transaction as link insert/delete. No history export or duplicate event per operation. Update architecture inventory and exact physical test incl nullable FK, preserve existing 42 entities. No production migration or Flyway.

## IDN-17 — Runtime role lifecycle management (W23/W26)

Status: PLANNED — registered for next execution after registration CI. Dependencies: accepted IDN-08, IDN-09, IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-15 and IDN-16. Baseline b105a9ea7813bd4b0d6c8ea08798503155085613, Build #72 success above. Residual W23 rename and W26 retire; existing role creation accepted under IDN-PLATFORM-01. Registration commit: `docs(identity): accept role composition and register role lifecycle`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): manage role lifecycle through HTTP`.

Business mechanism: administrators rename the display label of an existing enabled role or permanently retire it, independently of its configured name, without deleting definitions, links or grants. Reuse Role.rename/retire, PlatformRoleAdministrationService, PlatformRoleController, existing authorization and audit model. No new table/entity/service, no generic CRUD, reactivation, bulk edits or optimization. Stable role ID/code and platformRole discriminator remain immutable.

- Exact bearer HTTP operations: POST /v1/platform/roles/{roleId}/rename requires platform-role.rename, JSON {displayName,reason}; POST /v1/platform/roles/{roleId}/retire requires platform-role.retire, JSON {reason}. Positive role ID, nonblank label <=120, nonblank reason <=255; 200/no-store with existing PlatformRoleResponse {id,code,displayName,enabled,platformRole}. Unknown role generic 403 after operation authorization, invalid input 400, unauthenticated 401, unauthorized 403, retired role or unchanged label/repeated retirement 409, storage/evidence failure 503. No company selector or caller actor, code change, authority conversion or other methods; exact allowlist. Preserve GET metadata for retired roles and existing bundle consultation. Harden named Role methods to reject invalid label, unchanged rename, rename after retirement and repeat retirement without changing constructor or mapping. Service maps state conflict to existing RoleChangeRejected and controller safe problem response. Duplicate labels allowed; labels do not determine authority.
- Manifest v3 (hyflo-identity-capabilities-v3) adds only platform-role.rename and platform-role.retire to the prior 23 executable definitions, retaining exact earlier definitions/labels; add two exact constants to implemented set and exclusive catalog-write set. Fresh HTTP initial explicit subset 17 company/8 platform (25 definitions), original internal company-only 17 unchanged. Reconciliation remains additive without grants; no automatic upgrade to initialized accounts, no reopening setup. Existing populated schemas require a separate controlled provisioning mechanism; disposable fresh-schema development only.
- Both mutations READ_COMMITTED, existing exclusive LOCAL provider gate (actor User -> LoginSession -> provider -> actor grant/role/link locks), then target Role findForUpdate. Recheck exact live actor authorization after target lock and immediately before mutation/evidence. Shared provider locking of existing company grant writes serializes against role retirement, as do exclusive catalog mutations; no new locking infrastructure. Rename permitted for any enabled role including assigned platform roles: label-only changes cannot confer/revoke authority. Retirement disables role permanently; existing links/grants/history remain, current effective authorization excludes disabled role, subsequent grant/association rejected; no session revocation or cascading changes. Assigned platform roles with any non-ended direct PlatformRoleGrant (including future starts) cannot be retired until continuity and grant management exist; reuse countNonEndedForRole. To preserve IDN-08 initial grant protection, company retirement also rejects a role with any non-ended direct grant originally issued by the reserved disabled bootstrap attribution actor (exact username hyflo-bootstrap, including future start; no role-name check), using a narrow count query in UserRoleGrantRepository. No fixed protected business role name. Other company roles may retire with non-ended direct or group grants (authority reduced, history retained); group inheritance remains unadmitted. No general last-company-admin/recovery claim; interim initial-grant guard must be revisited with continuity workflows. No reenable or hard delete.
- Audit: reuse T_10_01_21 immutable RoleDefinitionEvent without column/FK changes. RENAMED_FROM and RENAMED are two snapshots of old and new labels, same operation correlation ID, actor, UTC instant and reason, permission null; capture old snapshot before rename, new after, persist both in same transaction. RETIRED one snapshot after disabling, permission null. Use explicit lifecycle factory methods (no arbitrary event code from caller), enforce valid state per event code (CREATED/permission/rename enabled; RETIRED disabled), persisted actor/role and existing evidence bounds; preserve prior permission event rules. Extend existing RoleDefinitionEventService with one explicit recordLifecycle method accepting validated lifecycle events (only three admitted codes, MANDATORY); no new entity or generic audit infrastructure. Two correlated rename snapshots intentionally retain the old label even for setup-created roles without CREATED evidence. Existing event rows unchanged.

## IDN-18 — Platform role assignment in User Management

Status: PLANNED — owner requested simpler role assignment through User Management and selected Next on 2026-11-26. Baseline 82a0afb67d13364b927774f6e07782a7474396e0, Build #74 success. Dependencies: accepted IDN-08, IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-15, IDN-16, IDN-17. Registration commit: `docs(identity): accept role lifecycle and register user role assignment`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): assign and revoke platform roles in user management`.

Minimum business mechanism: reuse existing User Management controller, PlatformRoleGrantService, User, Role and PlatformRoleGrant (T_10_01_20). Authorized operator selects an existing eligible user and a configured platform role; assigns, lists or revokes that grant. Existing company grant APIs remain unchanged. No new module/entity/table, independent approval process, account model, bulk CRUD, or workflow engine. Account creation and initial activation remain existing company operations before promotion. This is a bounded first completion of platform role assignment, not complete platform account lifecycle. The prior broader proposal to remove assigned-role composition restrictions or add platform account credential/lifecycle recovery is not included in this envelope: preserve all existing company target protections even after last grant revocation and all assigned-platform-role composition/retirement guards. These are explicit residuals to reconcile before claiming complete User Management; avoid silently weakening earlier safety rules. No global user discovery endpoint; the UI can use the already authorized company user selector.

- Exact HTTP additions in UserAdministrationController: GET /v1/identity/users/{userId}/platform-role-grants?afterId=0 (platform-user-role-grant.read); POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/platform-role-grants JSON {roleId,reason} (platform-user-role-grant.assign); POST /v1/identity/users/{userId}/platform-role-grants/{grantId}/revoke JSON {reason} (platform-user-role-grant.revoke). Use new validated AssignPlatformRoleGrantRequest, existing UserLifecycleRequest for revoke, explicit PlatformRoleGrantResponse DTO with id/userId/roleId/roleCode/validFrom/validTo/revokedAt/reason/correlationId/grantedBy/revocationReason/revocationCorrelationId/revokedBy. No company selector, caller actor, credential data or automatic role assignment. GET 200 bounded stable ascending-ID history page (100), POST 201/204, no-store; existing safe 400/401/403/409/503 policy. Unknown/reserved target or mismatched grant safe 403 after authority. Exact method allowlist, no other route.
- Manifest v4 adds only these three exact implemented permissions to existing v3 (17 company/11 platform =28). Fresh explicit HTTP initial subset grants all 11 platform permissions to installer-configured initial role, retains original company-only bootstrap subset. Reconciliation remains additive without granting permissions; no automatic upgrade/reopening setup, no production migration. Existing initialized-schema provisioning remains a separately admitted residual. Preserve existing provision method and bootstrap-only restriction.
- Existing LOCAL provider exclusive write gate extended to these grant mutations, same actor/session/provider/grant/link lock protocol, then target User and Role/grant locks; retain recipient User lock shared protocol with company writes. Recheck exact live permission and durable delegation at decision instant after any blocking locks. READ_COMMITTED; UTC microsecond instant; atomic commit, fail closed on lock/storage errors (no partial grant/evidence); no new lock infrastructure. Read REPEATABLE_READ, exact permission before target lookup/history. Eligible assignment target ACTIVE LOCAL HUMAN, supported persisted local credential, not reserved attribution actor; deny self assignment. Role enabled platform type, nonempty bundle of valid supported platform permissions only; actor must hold every permission in assigned role plus assign permission through current nonexpiring direct platform grants (not a customer role name); no grant amplification via finite source or unsupported/cross-authority permission. New grants effective immediately, no expiry in this minimum slice (explicit design choice; model retains existing finite/future history). Reject duplicate non-ended target-role grant including future grants. Revoke only matched non-ended grant, with actual actor/reason/correlation/time through existing domain revoke, preserve row and history; permit authorized remediation of future grants, reject already ended/revoked. No need to recheck actor's removed permission after own revocation; recheck before mutation.
- Continuity: a grant mutation must leave at least one eligible active LOCAL HUMAN (not reserved actor) with a supported persisted credential and all implemented platform capabilities through the union of current nonexpiring enabled platform role grants. Use ordinary repository projections/queries and existing platform authorization validity rules, including candidate-bound fail-closed rule; count only users whose actual effective resolver can authorize (not role names, counts of rows, future/finite authority or disabled roles). Check prospective state excluding revoked grant before mutation under exclusive provider gate; also verify assignment cannot make recipient exceed the existing 1000 candidate-history ceiling, including ended history. No mandatory second admin. Existing company mutation guards and assigned platform composition/retirement guards keep continuity against other admitted mutations; own password changes preserve supported credentials. Concurrent operations may be rejected on contention; demonstrate safe serialized outcomes or rolled-back conflict without losing continuity. No claim of recovery from lost passwords, direct DB edits or provider outage. Preserve role events and all existing history.

## IDN-19 — Group management and membership (W27–W31)

Status: PLANNED — owner Next, 2026-11-26. Baseline a7030d4f3c7c1fbbf90e9360e1dce3ee9b45c3b9, Build #76 success. Depends on accepted IDN-03, IDN-08, IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-15–18. Registration commit: `docs(identity): accept platform role assignment and register group management`. Implementation commit: `feat(identity): manage groups and user memberships through HTTP`.

Minimum business mechanism: administrators configure named groups and add/remove existing users through one Group Management screen. Reuse Group, GroupMembership, their existing repositories and GroupService/GroupMembershipService; use one GroupAdministrationController and explicit DTOs. Existing Group has a globally unique immutable code and no company owner; preserve this existing model, admit platform-authorized administration only, not company-wide group discovery/mutation. Groups are reusable platform-managed definitions, not platform roles. Group membership grants no permission by itself. No company ownership inference, organization hierarchy change, default groups, nested groups, bulk edit, generic CRUD, workflow engine or group role assignment/effective inheritance in this slice. Future IDN-20 candidate must explicitly preserve company-bound resource and foreign-recipient invariants before enabling inherited access; no arbitrary cross-company authority. Platform account lifecycle, assigned-platform-role composition and controlled provisioning remain separate explicit residuals, not silently resolved by selecting group management.

- Exact bearer endpoints and permissions: GET /v1/platform/groups?afterId=0 and GET /v1/platform/groups/{groupId} (platform-group.read); POST /v1/platform/groups JSON {code,displayName,reason} (platform-group.create); POST /v1/platform/groups/{groupId}/rename JSON {displayName,reason} (platform-group.rename); POST /v1/platform/groups/{groupId}/retire JSON {reason} (platform-group.retire); GET /v1/platform/groups/{groupId}/memberships?afterId=0 (platform-group-membership.read); POST /v1/platform/groups/{groupId}/memberships JSON {userId} (platform-group-membership.assign); POST /v1/platform/groups/{groupId}/memberships/{membershipId}/revoke, no business payload required (platform-group-membership.revoke). No company selector or actor from request. Use existing UserLifecycleRequest for retirement. Positive identifiers and nonnegative cursor, bounded stable ascending-ID pages of 100, no-store. GroupResponse {id,code,displayName,enabled}; GroupMembershipResponse {id,groupId,userId,validFrom,validTo,revokedAt,grantedBy,revokedBy,active}. Active denotes membership validity and enabled group at server evaluation instant, not effective access; retain retired/ended history, do not expose credentials/account details. Exact 200 reads/rename/retire, 201 create/assign, 204 revoke; safe 400/401/403/409/503 consistent with existing controllers. Unknown group/user/mismatched membership safe 403 after exact authority. ## IDN-21 — Own account profile consultation and email management

Status: PLANNED — owner Next/continue, 2026-11-26. Baseline 1e40f4c5c845bd4973a5d6c44851fc4d0b18be6f, Build #82 success. Dependencies: accepted IDN-02, IDN-04, IDN-05, IDN-10, IDN-11 and IDN-20 sequencing. Original IDN-11 / IDN-W07 residual, live IDN-21 (not a renumbering of historic design). Registration message: `docs(identity): accept inherited authorization and register own profile`. Implementation message: `feat(identity): consult own profile and change contact email`.

Minimum business mechanism: authenticated account reads its own profile and an eligible HUMAN account updates its own contact email using current local password re-verification. Reuse existing User email F_02, AuthenticationService, AuthenticationController, AuthenticationMapper, AuthenticationEvent, existing password encoder and bounded LoginAttemptLimiter. No new business configuration catalog or customer settings: user chooses contact email at runtime. Username, company, employee linkage, provider, type, status, credentials, roles and grants are not self-editable here. Email remains unverified contact data, never login identity, recovery authority, privilege or proof of mailbox ownership. No new mail delivery/verification or recovery workflow. Existing password/session management stays separate. Preserve /v1/me unchanged.

- File-owner prerequisite explicitly outstanding: User.profilePictureFileId (F_04) is a scalar reference; Foundation Infra File stores metadata/storage key but FileService has no ownership/authorized association or content-access contract. Do not expose an arbitrary file ID setter, file contents, storage key, or unvalidated reference. Profile picture upload/association/removal/readback remains an explicitly registered W07 residual pending selected Infra owner contract and appropriate reference validation/FK integration. No pretend full W07 closure. No cross-module access to Employee profile or modification of Employee email. No new entities, table, columns, cross-module imports or capability manifest changes.
- HTTP: GET /v1/me/profile returns 200 explicit OwnProfileResponse {userId,username,email,companyId,userTypeCode,userStatusCode}, live eligible LOCAL HUMAN or SERVICE account; POST /v1/me/profile/email accepts only {email,currentPassword}, returns 200 same DTO for changed or exact unchanged email. No userId/companyId selector or mass assignment, unknown body fields rejected with 400; no file field. Reject unexpected identity selectors on these operations (no target supplied); exact authenticated method allowlist; derive identity/session from SecurityContext and verify again in application. No company-grant prerequisite for own profile or platform privilege required; SERVICE read permitted, HUMAN only for change (403 otherwise). No reserved bootstrap account. No new permission, token claim or role seed. All responses no-store; safe 400/401/403/409/429/503, no SQL/email-owner/other account data or password in errors or DTO diagnostics. Use generic ACCOUNT_OPERATION_REJECTED for duplicate email only (MySQL duplicate key), 503 for evidence or other persistence failures. Rate-limit current password verification with existing direct peer/user/global budget; no new limiter or credential logs.
- Validate nonblank email, syntax and max 100 characters using existing Bean Validation conventions; reject reserved bootstrap email case-insensitively, whitespace and unsupported malformed request; preserve supplied valid email spelling (no silent normalization or case change), existing DB collation/UK_02 authoritative for collisions. No global email existence probe. Validate nonblank current password and existing bcrypt max 72 UTF-8 bytes before hashing; current supported credential belongs to caller. Same exact email is no-op only after successful password verification, no new success event. Add focused User.changeEmail named domain operation with required/length/reserved/active HUMAN invariants and existing field validation; no generic setter.
- Read transaction REPEATABLE_READ: requireOwnPrincipal, resolve live session/user through existing repository and ownsLiveSession, map safe projection inside transaction. Write READ_COMMITTED: authenticated context, lock User then current LoginSession then existing shared LOCAL provider gate, validate matching principal, live active session/account, provider and HUMAN eligibility before verification and again after potential blocking work; existing account lock serializes password changes, lifecycle, own revocation and concurrent profile updates. Use current credential check and no stale principal. Email update and evidence commit atomically; use DB uniqueness (including concurrent cross-account claim); no premature success on rollback. Preserve session/token validity on successful email update; username/password/hash, employee, picture, roles, grants and all other fields unchanged. Recheck live session immediately before mutation. No new session revocation or authorization widening.
- Existing AuthenticationEvent finite technical codes extend with PROFILE_EMAIL_CHANGED and PROFILE_EMAIL_FAILED (both <=24 characters), actor/user, provider, UTC microsecond timestamp and generated correlation. Record failed password confirmation after live eligibility check without recording submitted email or password; record success only for actual changed email. No previous/new email history stored, explicitly limited evidence; no new event table or generic Audit framework. Constraint/evidence failures roll back update and event; rejected duplicate has no committed success event. Preserve existing event semantics and constructor validation, no target-session FK for profile events.

## IDN-22 — Authorized Identity security history consultation

Status: PLANNED — selected for registration by owner Next, 2026-11-26. Exact reviewed baseline ad94a9a1beedb079f350284acb19d8e510aef067, Build #84 success. Historical IDN-W42 / original IDN-11 residual; live IDN-22 is new registered execution identity, not historical renumbering. Dependencies: accepted IDN-02/03/05/06/07/08/09/10/11/13, IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-15/16/17/18/19/20/21 as applicable to actual persisted evidence and authority. Registration message: `docs(identity): accept own profile and register security history`. Implementation message: `feat(identity): consult authorized security event history`.

Minimum business mechanism: read committed historical Identity security facts through seven explicit, bounded, read-only HTTP collections, separated into company-owned and platform-owned authority. Reuse six existing immutable event models, their save-only repositories (add only specific read methods), current live authorization services and capability registration. User-configurable roles and grants determine authorized readers; no role-name checks, caller actor selectors, universal administrator, new business catalog, search engine, audit entity, event duplication, export, aggregation, count, cross-module access or additional schema. Existing company and platform grant/membership history endpoints remain authoritative for those records, not replicated into a fabricated event stream. General Audit ownership and historical grant-revocation reason/correlation limitations remain separate.

- Company routes (exact GET methods, required positive companyId, optional afterId default 0, fixed ascending 100-row per-family keyset page, bare JSON list): `/v1/identity/security-history/authentication`, `/v1/identity/security-history/account-lifecycle`, `/v1/identity/security-history/employee-associations`. All require exact new company `security-history.read` through EffectiveAuthorizationService.requireCompanyPermission in the same read-only REPEATABLE_READ transaction; resolve live principal and company GLOBAL direct/eligible inherited permission before any history query or company existence disclosure. Use actual persisted event ownership: AuthenticationEvent joins its nonnull User and immutable owningOrganizationUnitId, excludes reserved hyflo-bootstrap; UserLifecycleEvent and UserEmployeeAssociationEvent filter recorded owningOrganizationUnitId AND target User immutable owner equality, exclude reserved bootstrap target; no current target active-state restriction (history survives suspension/disable), no current Employee/Organization private entity lookup, no actor-owner inference. Only the target's selected company history, never another company's records or unattributed logins. Actor ID is historical attribution, not authorization to inspect actor's separate account. No company grant from a platform role; no new foreign-recipient delegation exception.
- Platform routes (exact GET methods, optional afterId only, no companyId or target selector): `/v1/platform/security-history/role-definitions`, `/v1/platform/security-history/group-definitions`, `/v1/platform/security-history/capability-reconciliations`, `/v1/platform/security-history/unattributed-authentication`. Exact new independent `platform-security-history.read` via PlatformAuthorizationService.requireRead in the same read-only REPEATABLE_READ transaction, eligible live direct platform HUMAN authority only. RoleDefinitionEvent includes both platform and company role definition events because both are platform-managed shared definitions; GroupDefinitionEvent and CapabilityReconciliationEvent are platform-owned. Unattributed authentication returns ONLY AuthenticationEvent records with user IS NULL and exact LOGIN_FAILED code; no guessed username, email, account, session or company. No platform-wide read of attributed company authentication/lifecycle/employee events via this permission. Do not repurpose company authority or existing platform catalog read permission as history authorization. Explicit safe 400/401/403/503 and no-store; deny other HTTP methods and unsupported paths. Reject unexpected query selectors, repeated selector values, negative/overflow/malformed cursors before lookup, no request body or caller-supplied actor. Authorization precedes every protected read, even when a page would be empty; query scope in database, never retrieve global results then filter. Authorized empty list is not target-existence evidence. Read transaction snapshot semantics as in existing gates, no instantaneous in-flight cancellation claim.
- Explicit per-family immutable projections inside SecurityHistoryService and corresponding nested response records in SecurityHistoryResponse, mapped by SecurityHistoryMapper, never serialize domain/JPA objects. Common fields id, occurredAt (UTC Instant), correlationId; eventCode only where stored (capability event has no eventCode: fixed typed route, do not invent a stored code). Authentication adds eventCode, userId (known-company only), identityProviderCode, loginSessionId if present; no JWT, refresh secret/digest, username, email or credential metadata. Unattributed variant omits userId/loginSessionId entirely. Lifecycle adds eventCode, companyId, userId, performedByUserId, previousStatusCode, resultingStatusCode. Employee association adds eventCode, companyId, userId, performedByUserId, previousEmployeeId, resultingEmployeeId. Role definition adds eventCode, roleId, roleCode, displayName snapshot, platformRole, performedByUserId, nullable permissionId. Group definition adds eventCode, groupId, groupCode, displayName snapshot, enabled, performedByUserId. Capability reconciliation adds performedByUserId, manifestId, manifestDigest and stored insertedActionCodes/insertedResourceTypeCodes/insertedPermissionCodes as arrays decoded from validated newline-delimited snapshots; preserve historical values rather than recompute against current manifest. Keep null historical values, exact source IDs, UTC precision, correlated rename snapshots and retired definitions. Free-text reason intentionally omitted from the first read surface because it can contain caller-supplied sensitive data; controlled reason disclosure/export is an explicit residual, not claimed as complete forensic history. No raw secrets, login input, profile/contact data, arbitrary payloads or related account/employee details. DTO diagnostics must not print user-supplied labels, correlation or other history contents. Document precise response schema for each route with OpenAPI annotations if needed. No new event write during consultation.
- Repository query contracts: AuthenticationEventRepository.findCompanyPage(companyId,afterId,Pageable) and findUnattributedPage(afterId,Pageable); UserLifecycleEventRepository.findCompanyPage and UserEmployeeAssociationEventRepository.findCompanyPage with the same scoped arguments; RoleDefinitionEventRepository, GroupDefinitionEventRepository and CapabilityReconciliationEventRepository each findHistoryPage(afterId,Pageable). Explicit JPQL predicates and ORDER BY id ascending, PageRequest.of(0,100), no unbounded query/count. Use persisted IDs and event snapshots, map in the transaction; no lazy serialization or open-in-view. Retain all existing save methods, immutable event constructors, domain mappings and writes. No new table, column, association, database index or migration in this task; measurable query tuning deferred.
- Capability manifest v7: add exactly security-history.read and platform-security-history.read (40 total: 18 company, 22 platform), independent resource codes and existing read action, deterministic digest via current manifest mechanism. Add the platform code to implemented READ set, not catalog writes; maintain existing direct platform continuity policy. Add both to corresponding explicit bootstrap initial subsets; internal company-only synthetic setup now 18, fresh HTTP setup 18 company/22 platform. Existing initialized schema: reconciliation inserts definitions only, not role links or grants; no automatic privilege grant, no bypass of assigned platform composition guards, no production migration or recovery. Existing initialized-schema provisioning and continuity implications remain explicit prior residual; no claim of upgrade readiness. Existing direct/group delegation and foreign catalog-only allowlist remain unchanged. Runtime administrators configure eligible role associations/grants through existing authorized mechanisms.

## IDN-23 — Safe assigned-platform role composition and retirement

Status: PLANNED — owner Next selects registration, 2026-11-26. Exact baseline c1cf897d57db56e89f6c8f948f864e9ff2fd01b2, Build #87 success. Dependencies: accepted IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-15/16/17/18 and IDN-19/20/22 as applicable; preserve existing role, grant, company and group contracts. Registration message: `docs(identity): accept security history and register safe platform role changes`. Implementation message: `feat(identity): safely change assigned platform roles`. Historical W24/W25/W26 residual; live IDN-23 does not renumber original packages.

Minimum business mechanism: permit authorized runtime administrators to add/remove supported permissions and retire enabled platform roles with existing non-ended platform grants only when delegation and prospective administrator continuity are demonstrably safe. Roles and their bundles remain runtime configurable. Reuse existing three POST routes, Role, RolePermission, PlatformRoleGrant, immutable RoleDefinitionEvent, existing exact permissions and provider-serialized transactions. No new endpoint, permission, DTO, entity, schema, migration, generic policy framework or production upgrade. Manifest v7 remains 40 (18 company/22 platform). Keep existing unassigned role behavior, company role composition/retirement guards, direct grant assignment/revocation, role rename, resource scope and membership behavior unchanged.

- Existing routes: POST /v1/platform/roles/{roleId}/permissions/{permissionId}/associate, /remove, and POST /v1/platform/roles/{roleId}/retire. Exact independent permissions platform-role-permission.associate, platform-role-permission.remove, platform-role.retire before selected role/permission discovery; existing safe 400/401/403/409/503/no-store and success 204/204/200, existing explicit DTOs/reason and lifecycle evidence. No new routes or capability definitions. Role discriminator and code immutable; only enabled role may change; duplicate association/missing removal/repeated retire remain conflicts.
- Reuse PlatformAuthorizationService.requireCatalogWrite, READ_COMMITTED and existing actor User -> current LoginSession -> exclusive LOCAL provider -> actor grants/roles/permission links -> target Role and selected Permission/link locks. Keep exclusive provider across decision, write and evidence. It serializes admitted catalog/reconciliation/platform grant mutations and company writes; protected account lifecycle remains prohibited, own password change only replaces a supported hash with another supported hash (does not remove credential or disable user). Do not introduce locks on unrelated candidate User rows after provider acquisition; avoid inversion with existing actor/account lock order. Validate exact live actor/session and operation again after blocking locks and immediately before mutation; evaluate current microsecond UTC instant for grant validity/continuity; re-evaluate prospective conditions at final decision. If contention or evidence/persistence failure, safe rollback. No stale JWT roles or name-based administrators.
- If platform role has no non-ended grants retain existing role editing semantics. If it has any non-ended grant (future starts included; ended or revoked history alone does not restrict), require actor's durable exact operation under active nonexpiring direct platform grants with eligible LOCAL HUMAN user and supported credential; finite/future/ended/revoked/disabled/over-cap histories do not contribute. Association additionally requires actor already holds the exact added supported platform capability through durable authority before mutation, not prospective authority; no privilege amplification, including self/other grant holders. Reject non-ended future-start/finite/revoked-in-future synthetic/unsupported temporal grants for addition until separately admitted (existing future-grant denial retained). Add repository count query on selected role for non-ended grants whose validFrom > now, validTo is nonnull, or revokedAt is nonnull; no global grant scan or new data model. Existing associated bundle must not contain malformed, unsupported or company links when expanding an assigned platform role; empty bundle after legitimate removal can be repaired. Removals of malformed/unsupported links remain possible for remediation subject to operation and continuity; never delete the Permission definition. Retirement preserves all role permission links and grants/history. No physical role deletion or cascade.
- Prospective continuity: refactor existing PlatformAuthorizationService.durablePermissions into one shared calculation with optional excluded grant ID, excluded role ID (retirement) or exact excluded role/permission link (removal); retain existing 1000-candidate guard, live user/role checks, direct nonexpiring effective grant validity and structural permission validation for grant operations. Filter by exact link identity, not permission code globally; the same permission through another eligible role still counts. Exclude entire target role only for retirement. Reuse existing PlatformRoleGrantService credential-qualified durable administrator candidate search via package-private boolean helper used by existing assign/revoke continuity and PlatformRoleAdministrationService; preserve existing grant behavior and exception semantics. No new service or generic abstraction. Require an eligible supported-credential HUMAN with all 22 implemented platform permissions after the proposed removal/retirement; for assigned-role addition require existing continuity (addition of already-held supported capability cannot reduce it). No combination of partial users, group/company grants, retired/expired/future/finite/revoked candidates or unsupported credentials counts. For assigned-role removal/retirement actor must still possess the operation through prospective durable authority, preventing self-lockout of the authorizing operation; actor may deliberately lose other permissions provided continuity survives. Retain existing post-retirement live operation recheck and add equivalent post-mutation check for assigned role changes if needed, without allowing authority from the new link to authorize its own insertion. Evaluate before writes rather than depending on stale persistence context or implicit flush to simulate prospective state. Existing full-continuity requirement is not relaxed for old initialized manifests: no missing-capability bootstrap/recovery in this task.
