## IDN-25 — Protected platform-account lifecycle management

Status: PLANNED — owner approved registration only, 2026-11-26. Dependencies: accepted IDN-05/06/07 (account lifecycle, credentials, session revocation), IDN-PLATFORM-01, IDN-18, IDN-22 and IDN-23; IDN-24 explicitly deferred. Baseline 4ba9dc3472e07d8f605c8b470d9a2b0160fdc737, Build #91 success. Registration message: `docs(identity): defer upgrade provisioning and register protected account lifecycle`. Implementation message: `feat(identity): manage protected platform account lifecycle`. Preserve historical W-workflow IDs; this is the next unused sequential IDN identity, not a new baseline acceptance.

Minimum business mechanism: in the existing User Management screen/API, an independently authorized platform operator selects an existing protected account and suspends, reactivates, disables, resets an eligible existing credential, or revokes live sessions. Protected means any persisted PlatformRoleGrant history, including ended/revoked-only; no reclassification or return to company administrative control. Reuse User, UserStatus, LocalCredential, LoginSession, UserLifecycleEvent, UserAdministrationService and Controller, PlatformAuthorizationService and PlatformRoleGrantService; add no entity, DTO, new service, separate account registry, fixed role, workflow engine or upgrade escape hatch. Existing company-owned user selection/read and platform grant history remain unchanged; no global account discovery, new create/activate path, arbitrary employee/company transfer, permission/grant mutation or platform access to engineering/company data. Runtime administrators configure role permissions and select recipients. Promotion remains through accepted IDN-18, after ordinary account activation.

- Five independent bearer-only POST routes within existing UserAdministrationController: /v1/identity/users/{userId}/platform-account/suspend, /reactivate, /disable (UserLifecycleRequest, UserAccountResponse 200 via existing mapper); /platform-account/credential/reset (LocalCredentialRequest, 204); /platform-account/sessions/revoke (UserLifecycleRequest, 204). Exact corresponding permissions platform-user.suspend, platform-user.reactivate, platform-user.disable, platform-credential.reset, platform-login-session.revoke. No company selector, caller-controlled actor, token, role or status parameter. Existing error/no-store/ProblemDetail contract and exact method allowlist, 400 invalid request, 401 unauthenticated, 403 missing authority/unknown or ineligible target, 409 lifecycle/continuity conflict, 503 storage/lock/evidence failure. Reuse current controller execute/transition mapping and rejection classes. Reject secrets in reason, never echo password/hash/token. Add exact routes to IdentitySecurityConfiguration; do not relax default deny or existing company guards.
- Manifest v8 adds only these five implemented capabilities (45 total: 18 company/27 platform); add exact constants to PlatformAuthorizationService implemented and exclusive catalog-write sets, and explicit fresh HTTP bootstrap platform subset. Preserve existing 18 company-only synthetic initialization, all 40 previous definitions, labels/digest convention, reconciliation without grants and no changes to older initialized schemas. No new business status or new permission for general recovery. Initial role remains installer-configured and is not an authorization rule.
- Write READ_COMMITTED: exact live permission before protected target lookup; require eligible LOCAL HUMAN actor, supported credential and exact durable operation from direct current nonexpiring grants, not finite/group/company authority. Reuse exclusive LOCAL provider gate and actor User -> actor LoginSession -> LOCAL provider -> actor grant/role/link locks, then selected target User lock; recheck live session, exact operation and durable authority after blocking work, including password encoding, before mutation/evidence. Reuse current target user/session writer serialization against login, refresh, own password change, company writes, role/grant/membership mutation; safe rollback on lock inversion/contention rather than weakening locks. Do not lock unrelated candidate users after provider acquisition. Target must be distinct persisted nonreserved LOCAL HUMAN with actual platform grant history and enabled provider. Deny ordinary company-only accounts, SERVICE, reserved actor and self; retain all company mutation prohibitions for any platform history. Status rules: ACTIVE -> SUSPENDED, SUSPENDED -> ACTIVE only with supported existing credential, ACTIVE/SUSPENDED -> DISABLED terminal; no pending promotion/recovery. Reset existing credential only for ACTIVE/SUSPENDED (including unsupported existing hash repair with valid replacement); no credential initialization, same-password replacement, disabled target reset or status change. Reuse existing length/UTF-8/reason checks and encoder; suspend, disable and reset revoke every unrevoked target session under existing account lock (including expired unrevoked rows as existing policy); standalone revoke only actual live sessions, reject no live sessions, keep status unchanged. Refresh tokens cannot revive revoked sessions; no physical deletion of session/token/grant rows.
- Continuity: extend existing package-private PlatformRoleGrantService.hasDurableAdministrator helper with optional excluded user ID, filtering that identity before the same supported-credential/direct-nonexpiring complete-authority calculation; keep old overload and existing callers unchanged. Suspend/disable and credential reset require a complete eligible administrator other than target (all 27 platform permissions, actual effective resolver and 1000-history cap); reactivation/session revoke require existing complete administrator and no upgrade/recovery loophole. Exclude whole target identity before mutation, not individual role, and re-evaluate under provider lock before status/hash/session mutation. Reset also requires actor's existing supported credential and durable operation; administrative knowledge of target password is privileged, not ordinary platform scope.
- Credential-reset anti-amplification boundary (conservative engineering choice): actor must have direct active nonexpiring, nonrevoked grant of every enabled platform role in target's non-ended grant history, including future/finite rows; deny malformed/unsupported/cross-authority bundles and over-cap history, require exact role identity and supported bundle rather than role-name/permission-count comparison. This intentionally prevents a reset-only operator acquiring a more privileged principal. For every non-ended company grant of the target (including future grants), require GLOBAL scope with null resource ref, enabled supported company role, actor and target both owned by that exact company, and actor already holds the same direct company role in that company through active nonexpiring nonrevoked authority; require eligible company under existing owner contract and structurally valid supported company links. Reject unsupported resource/foreign/malformed authority or unproved coverage; no company inheritance is available to a platform-history target under current EffectiveAuthorizationService. Add one bounded query in UserRoleGrantRepository for target non-ended company grant rows across all scopes/companies (max 1001, fail closed above 1000); use existing per-company candidate query for actor, and existing bounded PlatformRoleGrantRepository.findCandidates and RolePermissionRepository.findForRoles. Never ignore future grants or infer equality from labels. Revalidate the coverage at final decision under exclusive provider/target locks. This deliberately blocks resetting an initial administrator whose unique nonexpiring company role cannot be covered by the actor, and does not permit an operator to obtain new company authority via reset. Preserve legitimate own-password change; do not invent an exception to grant restrictions. Reuse existing trusted administrator-supplied password handover semantics; this is not independent target identity proof, secret delivery, forced-password-change, credential recovery when no complete administrator exists, or a guarantee against a trusted operator retaining a supplied secret. Stronger target-controlled recovery and uncovered privileged resets remain separately admitted security/production-preparation residuals. Do not broaden company reset semantics or introduce a new recovery token workflow here.
- Evidence: reuse existing immutable UserLifecycleEvent SUSPENDED/REACTIVATED/DISABLED/CREDENTIAL_RESET/SESSIONS_REVOKED, actual target owner snapshot, previous/resulting status, authenticated distinct actor, UTC time, reason and correlation, one event in same transaction. No new event type or schema. Existing company-scoped IDN-22 history retains target company attribution; no new platform history export. Roll back target status, credential hash/time, sessions and event together on failure. Keep role/grant/group histories untouched; no automatic deletion or revocation of grants on suspension/disable. No new account transfer, credential retrieval, notification or out-of-band identity proof.

## IDN-26 — Ensure current credentials at the login serialization boundary

Status: PLANNED — selected for registration by owner Next, 2026-11-26. Dependency: IDN-02, IDN-04, IDN-10/11, IDN-25 bounded acceptance; baseline dd26f05d33adfb37390221aebe4d0d6dc30d4218, Build #95 success. This is a small security-correctness correction, not a new business workflow. Current AuthenticationService.login uses default @Transactional, first performs an ordinary IdentityProviderRepository.findByCode read, then UserRepository.findForAuthentication (PESSIMISTIC_WRITE), then ordinary LocalCredentialRepository.findByUser_Id. On MySQL default REPEATABLE READ the initial consistent read can establish a snapshot before a concurrent reset commits; acquiring the User lock does not advance that snapshot. Thus an old credential hash may be read after the reset's session revocation transaction. Build #95 concurrent scenario passed but did not force that particular snapshot ordering. No runtime exploitation or failed test claimed. Preserve the existing User-lock serialization and all existing local authentication, bcrypt, limiter, session/refresh, status, error and event semantics. Minimum correction: explicitly use @Transactional(isolation = Isolation.READ_COMMITTED) for login, matching the established refresh/password-change transaction pattern; no datasource-wide isolation override, extra locks, new permission, table, DTO, endpoint, dependency or generic framework. Keep ordinary authorization and business configuration unchanged.

Registration/acceptance commit: `docs(identity): accept lifecycle verification and register login consistency correction`. Exact registration scope 0 creates / 1 update (`docs/ROADMAP.md`) / 0 deletes. Implementation commit: `fix(identity): read current credential after login account lock`. Exact implementation scope 0 creates / 3 updates / 0 deletes:
- `src/main/java/dz/sh/hyflo/modules/foundation/identity/application/service/AuthenticationService.java` — login transaction isolation only (existing Isolation import); preserve every other method and all locks.
- `src/test/java/dz/sh/hyflo/IdentityBootstrapIT.java` — one focused real MySQL regression, use existing setup, password encoder, TransactionTemplate and actual protected reset route. Deterministically establish an ordinary provider read in a test transaction using the login method's declared Spring transaction isolation, commit an actual authorized password reset on another connection via HTTP, then invoke the actual login service in the first transaction. Assert old password rejected, no newly issued session/refresh token, retained reset evidence, and successful fresh HTTP login with replacement. The transaction is synthetic ordering instrumentation (not a claim of a forced HTTP row-lock waiter); the real existing HTTP concurrent login/refresh/reset scenario remains unchanged. Verify actual isolation and no false acceptance on DB errors. Use exact existing test helpers and no timing-only sleeps, external grants, root DB access, production test hooks or additional test frameworks. If the proposed deterministic method cannot be implemented with existing tooling, report that limitation and retain acceptance pending rather than substituting source inspection for MySQL evidence.
- `docs/ROADMAP.md` — append-only implementation submission and evidence limitations.

Acceptance: preserve all 22 unit and 32 real MySQL integration methods; add one regression (expected 33 IT), zero failures/errors/skips, existing login/refresh/reset/rollback/expiry cases pass; exact-SHA HyFloAPI Build, retained tests/JaCoCo/SBOM/JAR. No requirement for additional production security features or full threat-model expansion. No production-schema upgrade, deployment or release. Next business candidate after acceptance: Organization management. Execute implementation only from accepted registration HEAD on owner Next. Stop after observing applicable registration CI startup.

#### Current persistent fields and physical allocation

The source inventory covers 43 entities (Identity 23, Organization 12, Reference 7, Infra 1), 43 typed owner repositories and 320 physical column placements including keys and versions. Each model is in `modules.foundation.<owner>.domain.model` and each repository is in the corresponding `infrastructure.persistence.repository` package. All 43 tables declare an identity-generated `Long id` at `F_00`. The current entity classes declare their own mappings without mapped superclass inheritance. Historical `GenericModel` provided `F_00 id` and `A_01 version` to the models that extended it, and the legacy joined `Employee` inherited the operational fields of `Person`. Both current uses of the `AuthorizationScope` embeddable are expanded in the grant rows.

Column numbers in the table denote `F_NN` unless shown in full as `A_01`, the optimistic-lock column. `!` indicates mapping requiredness, `?` indicates nullable, and `*` indicates the JPA mapping marks the column non-updatable. `String(n)` reports the declared length and bracketed text reports an explicit SQL type. `#NN` indicates a named same-module FK (`<table>_FK_NN`) from the listed column to the Java target entity's `F_00`. These are to-one links with many-to-one cardinality unless uniqueness on the referencing column limits it to one. Unique keys use `<table>_UK_NN`, with their column numbers listed in parentheses. Individual nullable localized names can be subject to the stronger constructor requirement that at least one valid name is supplied. Scalar owner references and the additional SQL-managed FK are detailed after the tables.

#### Owner references and persistence constraints

| Owner boundary or invariant | Current source evidence and disposition |
|---|---|
| Identity account → employee | `User.F_03` is an optional scalar with one-account-per-employee uniqueness (`T_10_01_08_UK_03`). `UserEmployeeReferenceConstraint` additionally manages/verifies the real restrictive MySQL `T_10_01_08_FK_04` to `T_10_02_09.F_00`. The selected Organization owner contract applies assignment/company eligibility for protected association. The persisted event's previous and resulting employee IDs are historical scalar evidence, with no FK. |
| Identity company and resource boundaries | `User.F_05`, company grants' `F_06` and lifecycle/association event company IDs are scalar. A COMPANY-classified OrganizationUnit supplies the company boundary; the admitted Organization service checks the company classification for scope, with the protected workflows applying their registered eligibility. These scalar mappings have no physical company FK. Grant `F_07` is absent for company-wide GLOBAL and holds a positive scalar for REGION/PIPELINE/INFRASTRUCTURE. External resource registration and company-ownership proof remain W37/W39 dependencies. Preserve separate platform role grants and the accepted fail-closed behavior. |
| Employee → Reference and Infra | `Employee.F_15/F_16` are optional Locality IDs, `F_17` an optional Country ID for nationality and `F_18` an optional Infra File ID. Current profile mapping requires positive IDs when supplied, with no database FK to those owners. Resolve owner existence and eligibility before exposing writes. `User.F_04` is a distinct optional profile File ID with no mapped FK. W07 remains deferred pending the Infra file workflow. |
| Organization hierarchy and assignment | A unit has one optional parent and required type/status. One employee can have dated assignment history. Each assignment belongs to one employee, one unit and one position with required type/status. The unique employee/start-date tuple prevents duplicate starts. Employee locking and overlap checking in the assignment service enforce the one-effective-assignment rule beyond the database unique key. Start is inclusive and end exclusive at `LocalDate` granularity; open end is allowed and employment dates bound assignment. Position reuse across units is intentional. |
| Reference geography and units | Country code uniqueness is global; State, District and Locality codes are unique within their required parents. Geography, Language and MeasurementUnit persist active state. Location has required finite WGS84 latitude and longitude in decimal degrees, optional finite elevation in metres above mean sea level and an optional Locality. Unit code is a stable unique key and symbol is case-sensitive display data. No certified survey datum or unit-conversion semantics are inferred. |
| Identity lifecycles and evidence | Required/optional JPA mappings above coexist with model rules and service checks for statuses, validity windows, enabled flags, revocation, token rotation and transaction isolation. Login and event instants are `Instant`; account transition dates are represented in attributable lifecycle events rather than repeated status date fields on User. The application histories have bounded Identity semantics. W41 still owns general Audit integration. |
| Infra metadata | Files store a unique opaque key, original filename, media type, size in bytes (at least zero) and creation instant. Physical presence, content integrity and resource permission need the owning content workflow. |

#### Comparison and disposition

| Area | Historical HyFloAPI | HidraAPI reference | DM-C1 disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| Employee and inherited Person | `Employee.registrationNumber` maps to `employeeNumber`. `Person` supplied Arabic/Latin names, birth date and places, addresses, localities, country and picture. Those fields are on the current Employee, with nationality explicit and employment dates, contact data and type/status added. | `Employee` has equivalent core identity/contact data and hire/termination instants. It also has separate display names and FR/EN birthplace slots. `EmployeeAddress` stores dated typed address rows, street lines, postal snapshot and primary address. | Equivalent core profile. Partial for structured/address history, separately curated display labels and translated birthplace variants. Keep one Arabic/Latin profile and day-level employment dates. Keep nationality and employee picture. Require a concrete profile/address workflow before adding a new aggregate or timezone/instant semantics. |
| Organization unit and position | `Structure`, `StructureType`, and `Job` map by business meaning to OrganizationUnit, OrganizationUnitType and Position. Parent, stable codes and labels are retained. Earlier job-to-structure and employee-to-job links are expressed through dated assignments. | Equivalent unit hierarchy, position levels, statuses and localized descriptions; HidraAPI adds a separate unit kind and uses instant validity. | Preserve COMPANY as an OrganizationUnitType code. A separate Company entity and OrganizationUnitKind stay excluded. Preserve current date precision under the one-effective-assignment rule. Keep appointed position distinct from Identity role. |
| Appointment, responsibility and shift | The earlier direct job assignment did not carry current dated transfer history. The old Employee.role association did not distinguish posts from access grants. | `EmployeeAssignment` adds appointment version, binding/policy IDs and revisions and event ID. Reporting lines, contact points, snapshots, operational scopes, shifts, responsibilities and delegations have separate records. | Equivalent basic employee/unit/position assignment, richer governed appointment and shift models absent. Preserve the accepted atomic transfer. Admit those records only with a selected business workflow, approval policy, and accepted owner boundaries. |
| Local account and credential | Earlier User records held login, email, password and account flags; Employee was linked directly. Current User/LocalCredential and statuses separate those responsibilities. | Equivalent core account, local hash and change time. HidraAPI stores display name, login counters and lock/time fields, and independent activation/suspension/disable instants. Session and authentication records also carry last-seen, IP/client, protocol and failure/risk fields. | Equivalent accepted LOCAL use cases. Partial for persistent lock counters, device/evidence enrichment and additional account projections. Preserve state and session histories, eligible actor checks and the registered lost-authority/credential deferrals. Revisit further evidence only with a privacy and retention purpose. |
| Roles, groups and authorization | Code/name catalogs, resource/action permissions and many-to-many role/group links precede current associations. Current RolePermission, GroupMembership and scoped role grants carry explicit links and lifecycle evidence. Legacy standalone Resource, catalog descriptions and resource-type active/protected markers have no one-to-one equivalents. | Role/group and permission definitions, direct/inherited grants and scoped membership overlap. Additional policy versions, expressions, direct permissions, delegation/approval references, external mappings and per-decision evidence have different authority semantics. | Partial by design. Retain the accepted capability manifest, supported resource types, temporal role grants and local provider. Missing external identity, policy ABAC data and approval-backed delegation are outside the selected scope. Future resource registration belongs with its owning modules. Retain the current label models pending the selected localization scope. |
| Geography and point locations | Country, State, District, Locality and Location map by meaning. The current required Country→State hierarchy and active/postal data extend the legacy catalog. Historical Zone and the ordered infrastructure Coordinate sequence have no direct Foundation equivalent. | AdministrativeState/District/Locality overlap on names, local codes, parent hierarchy, status and postal code. HidraAPI organization geography does not establish a global Country catalog or a shared Location contract. | Keep the Reference-owned Country and point Location contract. Extents, ordered alignment coordinates and route geometry require an Engineering owner. Do not treat historical Zone as a new geography or authorization scope without its business definition. |
| Languages and engineering units | No standalone equivalent catalog was identified in the selected legacy Foundation packages. | Document versions carry a language code. Engineering and operational mappings carry contextual unit declarations, which are separate from a shared catalog with conversions. | Keep the accepted Language and MeasurementUnit models. No User preferredLanguageId, QuantityType, conversion engine or reference-condition fields in this assessment. Later owner inputs must specify where engineering quantities and base conditions are authoritative. |
| File metadata and controlled document content | Legacy File stored extension, size, path and file type. Current opaque key, original filename, byte size and media type cover the metadata use. An extension can be obtained from an original name when present; no standalone extension column is needed for the current use. | `DocumentStorageObject` has provider/container, key/URI, size/type, encryption, checksum and verification state. `DocumentVersion` adds original filename, extension, language, uploader, lifecycle and approval evidence. | Partial overlap at metadata level. Preserve the Infra storage boundary and distinct document-version ownership. Revisit content integrity/hash verification once an Infra storage contract or controlled Documents workflow requires it. No upload, retrieval, revision or W07 acceptance inferred. |

#### Assessment decision and validation

- No new fields, tables or schema-only implementation envelope are supported by the selected Foundation workflows. Cross-owner existence/eligibility, protected appointment controls, file content integrity and richer security audit evidence need their corresponding owner contracts. Register the exact files and acceptance when an owner selects a concrete workflow. For any new relation, state requiredness, whether and where a physical FK is supported, lifecycle/deletion policy and positive and negative persistence scenarios. Preserve existing classifications, immutable evidence, accepted Identity outcomes, owner exclusions and historical numeric identifiers.
- Current allocations are `T_10_00_01`, `T_10_01_01–23`, `T_10_02_01–12` and `T_10_03_01–07`. The static inventory found 43 unique current table codes, consecutive business columns within each table and no collision with identifiers in the inspected legacy branch's Java tree. No allocation or renumbering is proposed. `A_01` is technical concurrency data and is shown only to reconcile the mapping.
- `HyFloApiIT` has a MySQL metamodel/repository check for all 43 entities, assignment transfer and overlap cases, geography/point/unit/file metadata readback, scoped-grant evidence and a dedicated scalar employee FK verifier. `HyFloApiTest` checks model rejection cases. Identity-focused ITs cover accepted flows and the IDN-26 regression. Existing source settings use default schema validation and disposable test `create-drop`; dev update is separate. No Flyway migration or runtime OpenAPI change is in this scope. Source inventory and static checks do not independently establish every physical constraint at runtime. Local environment provides Java 21 and lacks Docker. Hosted HyFloAPI Build on the assessment SHA remains the mandatory acceptance gate.
